### Responsories (650)

- `proprium-de-tempore/hebdomadae-augusti.json`
  - `hebdomadae-augusti, feria, feria-ii, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** O Lord, Father and Governor of my life, leave me not, lest I fall before mine adversaries, **responsum:** and mine enemy rejoice over me. **versus:** Take hold of shield and buckler, and stand up for mine help.
  - `hebdomadae-augusti, feria, feria-iii, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** My son, give me thine heart, and let thine eyes observe my ways. **responsum:** For they shall be an ornament of grace unto thine head. **versus:** My son, attend unto my wisdom, and incline thine ear unto my sayings.
  - `hebdomadae-augusti, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** God possessed me in the beginning, before He made the earth, before He created the depths, before He caused the fountains of water to spring. **responsum:** Before the mountains were settled, before there were any hills, did the Lord beget me. **versus:** When He prepared the heavens, I was there with Him, ordering all things.
  - `hebdomadae-augusti, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** I alone compassed the circuit of heaven, and walked on the waves of the sea. In every nation and in every people, I held the first place. **responsum:** In the greatness of my strength have I trodden under my feet the necks of such as be haughty and proud. **versus:** I dwell in the highest places, and my throne is in a cloudy pillar.
  - `hebdomadae-augusti, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** O send out wisdom from the throne of thy glory, O Lord, to be with me, and to labour with me, **responsum:** That I may know at all times what is pleasing unto thee. **versus:** Give me wisdom, O Lord, that sitteth by thy throne.
  - `hebdomadae-augusti, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Give me wisdom, O Lord, that sitteth by thy throne, and reject me not from among thy children. **responsum:** For I am thy servant and son of thine handmaid. **versus:** O send her out from the throne of thy glory, to be with me and to labour with me.
  - `hebdomadae-augusti, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** The fear of the Lord is the beginning of wisdom. **responsum:** A good understanding have all they that do His commandments. His praise endureth for ever. **versus:** Love is the keeping of her laws, for all wisdom is the fear of the Lord.
  - `hebdomadae-augusti, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Lord, remove far from me vanity and lies. **responsum:** Give me neither poverty nor riches, but feed me with food convenient for me. **versus:** Two things have I required of thee; deny me them not before I die.
  - `hebdomadae-augusti, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** O Lord, Father and God of my life, leave me not to evil counsels; give me not a proud look, but turn away from me an haughty mind, O Lord turn away from me concupiscence, **responsum:** And give me not over unto an impudent and froward mind, O Lord! **versus:** Leave me not, O Lord, lest mine ignorance increase, and my sins abound.
  - `hebdomadae-augusti, feria, feria-ii, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Great are thy judgments, O Lord, and thy words cannot be expressed. **responsum:** Thou didst make thy people mighty and honourable. **versus:** Thou broughtest them through the Red Sea, and leddest them through much water.
  - `hebdomadae-augusti, feria, feria-ii, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Lord, thine eyes behold all that is in the heart of man, and in thy book are they all written. **responsum:** Man looketh on the outward appearance, but God looketh on the heart. **versus:** For He searcheth all hearts, and understandeth all the imaginations of the thoughts.
- `proprium-de-tempore/hebdomadae-novembris.json`
  - `hebdomadae-novembris, feria, feria-ii, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** He hath redeemed His people, and ransomed them therefore they shall come and sing in the height of Zion, and shall rejoice in the goodness of the Lord, for wheat, and for wine, and for oil **responsum:** And they shall hunger no more. **versus:** And their soul shall be as a watered garden.
  - `hebdomadae-novembris, feria, feria-iii, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Before the face of thine anger, O God, the whole earth is troubled; **responsum:** But Thou, O Lord, have mercy, and make not an end utterly. **versus:** Lord our Ruler, how excellent is thy Name in all the earth!
  - `hebdomadae-novembris, feria, feria-iii, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Fence Thou this city; O Lord, and let thine angels keep the walls thereof. **responsum:** O Lord, hearken unto thy people with mercy. **versus:** O Lord, let thine anger be turned away from thy people, and from thine holy city.
  - `hebdomadae-novembris, feria, feria-iii, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** O Lord God! have mercy upon **responsum:** The sinful nation, upon the people laden with iniquity. **versus:** Let it repent thee concerning the transgression of thy people.
  - `hebdomadae-novembris, feria, feria-iv, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** I will show thee, O man, what is good, and what doth the Lord require of thee, but to **responsum:** Do justice and judgment, and to walk humbly with thy God. **versus:** Trust in the Lord, and do good, and dwell in the land.
  - `hebdomadae-novembris, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** I saw the Lord sitting upon a throne, high and lifted up, and the whole earth was full of His glory; **responsum:** And His train filled the temple. **versus:** Above it stood the Seraphim each one had six wings.
  - `hebdomadae-novembris, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Look down, O Lord, from the dwelling-place of thine holiness, and take thought for us. O my God, incline thine ear, and hear. **responsum:** Open thine eyes, and behold our desolation. **versus:** Give ear, O Shepherd of Israel, Thou That leadest Joseph like a flock.
  - `hebdomadae-novembris, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Consider, O Lord, how that the city sitteth solitary that was full of riches; how is she become as a widow, she that was great among the nations; **responsum:** She hath none to comfort her, save thee, O our God. **versus:** She weepeth sore in the night, and her tears are on her cheeks.
  - `hebdomadae-novembris, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** I have set watchmen upon thy walls, O Jerusalem; **responsum:** Which shall never hold their peace day nor night, to praise the name of the Lord. **versus:** They shall proclaim My might unto the nations, and declare My glory unto the Gentiles.
  - `hebdomadae-novembris, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Hedge us about with thy wall that cannot be broken down, O Lord, and shield us continually with the arms of thy might. **responsum:** O Lord God of Israel, deliver them that cry unto thee. **versus:** Deliver us also according to thy marvellous works, and give glory to thy Name.
  - `hebdomadae-novembris, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** We looked for peace, and it came not we asked for good, and behold trouble. We acknowledge, O Lord, our wickedness. **responsum:** Forget us not for ever. **versus:** O Lord (our God) we have sinned, we have done ungodly, we have dealt unrighteously in all thine ordinances.
  - `hebdomadae-novembris, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Blessed is the people **responsum:** Whom the Lord of hosts hath blessed, saying O Israel thou art the work of Mine own hands, thou art Mine own inheritance. **versus:** Blessed is the nation whose God is the Lord, and the people whom He hath chosen for His own inheritance.
  - `hebdomadae-novembris, feria, feria-ii, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** I am straitened on every side, and know not what to choose. **responsum:** It is better for me to fall into the hands of men, than to sin against the law of my God. **versus:** For if I do this thing, it is death unto me and if I do it not, I cannot escape your hands.
  - `hebdomadae-novembris, feria, feria-ii, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** The Lord hath sent His angel, and hath shut the lions' mouths, that **responsum:** They have not hurt me forasmuch as before Him innocency was found in me. **versus:** God hath sent forth His mercy and His truth, (and delivered) my soul from among the lions' whelps.
- `proprium-de-tempore/hebdomadae-octobris.json`
  - `hebdomadae-octobris, feria, feria-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Judas said unto Simon his brother: Choose thee out men, and go, and deliver thy brethren that are in Galilee and I, and Jonathan thy brother, will go into the country of Galaad. **responsum:** As the Will is in heaven, so let it be. **versus:** Arm yourselves, ye valiant men, and be in readiness for it is better for us to die in battle, than to behold the calamities of our people, and our sanctuary.
  - `hebdomadae-octobris, feria, feria-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** This is a lover of the brethren, and of the people of Israel **responsum:** This is one who prayeth much for the people, and for all the Holy City, Jerusalem. **versus:** There appeared a man most gentle toward all his people.
  - `hebdomadae-octobris, feria, feria-iii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Thou, O Lord of all things, Who hast need of nothing, wast pleased that the Temple of thine habitation should be among us. **responsum:** Therefore now, O Lord, keep this house ever undefiled **versus:** Thou, O Lord, didst choose this house, that thy Name should be called on therein, and to be an house of prayer and petition for thy people.
  - `hebdomadae-octobris, feria, feria-iii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Open thine eyes, O Lord, and behold our affliction for the heathen are come round about us to punish us. **responsum:** But Thou, O Lord, stretch forth thine arm, and deliver our souls. **versus:** Punish them that oppress us and with pride do us wrong, and keep thine own portion.
  - `hebdomadae-octobris, dominica, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** The Lord open your hearts in His law and commandments, and send peace in your days. **responsum:** May He grant you salvation and redeem you out of all evil. **versus:** The Lord hear your prayers, and be at one with you, and never forsake you in the time of trouble.
  - `hebdomadae-octobris, dominica, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** The Lord hear your prayers, and be at one with you, and never forsake you in the time of trouble **responsum:** Even He, the Lord our God. **versus:** Give you all an heart to serve Him, and to do His will.
  - `hebdomadae-octobris, dominica, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Our enemies are gathered together, and make their boast of their own strength. O Lord, break their power, and scatter them **responsum:** That they may know that there is none other that fighteth for us, but only Thou, O our God! **versus:** Scatter them in thy strength, and destroy them, O Lord our Shield.
  - `hebdomadae-octobris, dominica, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Be ye not afraid of the assault of the enemy; remember how our fathers were delivered. **responsum:** Now, therefore, let us cry unto heaven, and our God will have mercy upon us. **versus:** Remember His marvellous works that He hath done unto Pharaoh and his host in the Red Sea.
  - `hebdomadae-octobris, dominica, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** The heathen are assembled together to fight against us, and we know not what we should do. **responsum:** Our eyes look unto thee, O Lord our God, that we should not perish. **versus:** What things they imagine against us, Thou knowest. How shall we be able to stand against them, except Thou be our help.
  - `hebdomadae-octobris, dominica, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Thine, O Lord, is the power, thine is the kingdom, O Lord, and Thou art exalted above all the heathen. **responsum:** Give peace in our time, O Lord. **versus:** O Lord God, Creator of all things, Who art fearful and strong, righteous and merciful.
  - `hebdomadae-octobris, dominica, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** The sun shone upon the shields of gold, and the mountains glistered therewith; **responsum:** And the army of the heathens was spread abroad. **versus:** For the army was very great and mighty then Judas and his host drew near and entered into battle.
  - `hebdomadae-octobris, feria, feria-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** They decked the fore-front of the Temple with crowns of gold, and dedicated the Altar unto the Lord. **responsum:** And there was very great gladness among the people. **versus:** They praised the Lord with Psalms and thanksgiving.
  - `hebdomadae-octobris, feria, feria-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** They praised the Lord with psalms and thanksgiving **responsum:** Who had done so great things in Israel, and given them the victory the Lord Almighty. **versus:** They decked the fore-front of the Temple with crowns of gold, and dedicated the Altar unto the Lord.
- `proprium-de-tempore/hebdomadae-septembris.json`
  - `liber-job, feria, feria-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** My harp is turned to mourning, and my organ into the voice of them that weep. **responsum:** Let me alone, O Lord, for my days are vanity. **versus:** My skin is black upon me, and my bones are burned with heat.
  - `liber-esther, incipit-libri, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** O my Lord, the King Almighty All things are in thy power, and there is no man that can gainsay thy will; **responsum:** Deliver us for thy Name's sake. **versus:** Hear our prayer, and turn our sorrow into joy.
  - `liber-judith, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Strengthen me, O King, Who reignest over the holy ones. **responsum:** Put Thou in my mouth clear and well-sounding words. **versus:** O Lord, King of all forces, turn back their device upon themselves.
  - `liber-esther, incipit-libri, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** I have had no hope in any other but in thee, O God of Israel, Who art angry, and wilt again show mercy, **responsum:** Thou that forgivest all the sins of men when they are in affliction. **versus:** O Lord God, Maker of heaven and earth, look upon our low estate;
  - `liber-esther, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Remember me for good, O Lord God, **responsum:** And put not away my works of mercy, which I have wrought in the house of my God, and in the times of His solemn rites. **versus:** Remember me, O Lord my God
  - `liber-tobiae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** We have heard of the tribulation of those cities, which they have suffered, and we have fainted. Fear and confusion of mind are fallen upon us. Even the mountains will not give us a refuge. **responsum:** Lord, have mercy. **versus:** We have sinned like our forefathers, we have done unjustly, and wrought iniquity.
  - `liber-judith, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** The Lord bless thee by His power, Who hath brought our enemies to nought through thee. **responsum:** And may the praise of thee never fail from the mouth of men. **versus:** Blessed be the Lord, Who hath created the heaven and the earth, because that He hath so glorified thy name this day.
  - `liber-judith, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** We know no strange God before the Lord. In Him we trust. **responsum:** He despiseth us not, neither putteth He away His salvation from our nation. **versus:** His mercy let us seek with tears, and humble our souls before Him.
  - `liber-job, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** What shall we receive good at the hand of God, and shall we not receive evil? **responsum:** The Lord gave and the Lord hath taken away. As the Lord hath pleased, so hath it befallen. Blessed be the Name of the Lord. **versus:** Naked came I out of my mother's womb, and naked shall I return thither.
  - `liber-job, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** My sighing cometh before I eat, and my roarings are poured out like the waters, for the thing which I greatly feared is come upon me, and that which I was afraid of is come unto me. Was not I silent? Held not I my peace? Was not I at rest? **responsum:** And trouble came. **versus:** Behold, I cannot help myself, and they that were needful unto me have forsaken me.
  - `liber-job, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Why do ye argue against the words of truth? Do ye imagine words to reprove me and strive to confound one that is your friend? **responsum:** Nevertheless, finish that ye have in mind. **versus:** Judge that which is just, and ye shall find no iniquity in my tongue.
  - `liber-job, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** My flesh is clothed with worms and clods of dust. My skin is dry and drawn together. **responsum:** Remember me, O Lord, for my life is wind. **versus:** My days are swifter than a weaver's shuttle, and are spent without hope.
  - `liber-job, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** My days are few, and in a short while they will be ended. Let me alone, then, O Lord, that I may bewail my sorrow a little; **responsum:** Before I go to the land of darkness and of the shadow of death. **versus:** Thine hands, O Lord, have made me, and fashioned me together round about, and yet dost Thou forthwith destroy me.
  - `liber-job, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Hide not thy face from me, O Lord. Withdraw not thine hand far from me, **responsum:** And let not thy dread make me afraid. **versus:** O Lord, correct me but in mercy not in thine anger, lest Thou bring me to nothing.
  - `liber-job, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** O that Thou wouldest hide me in the grave; that Thou wouldest keep me secret, until thy wrath be past, even thine, O Lord, Thou That alone art God; **responsum:** That Thou wouldest appoint me a set time, and remember me! **versus:** Are thy days as the days of man, that Thou inquirest after mine iniquity, and there is none that can deliver out of thine hand.
  - `liber-judith, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** O Adonai, O Lord God, Thou art great and glorious. Who hast given salvation into the hand of a woman: **responsum:** Graciously hear the prayers of thy servants. **versus:** Blessed art Thou, O Lord, Who failest none that put their trust in thee, and humblest such as boast themselves in their own strength.
  - `liber-judith, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** O Lord, Ruler of the heavens and of the earth, Maker of the waters, King of every creature, **responsum:** Graciously hear the prayer of thy servants. **versus:** Thou, O Lord, unto Whom the supplications of the humble and meek are alway well-pleasing
  - `liber-judith, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** O Lord God, That breakest the battles from of old, lift up thine arm against the Gentiles, that devise evil against thy servants. **responsum:** And let thy right hand be glorified in us. **versus:** Throw down their strength in thy power, and bring down their force in thy wrath.
  - `liber-tobiae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** I entreat thee, O Lord, that thou wouldest loose me from this reproach, or that thou wouldest take me away from the earth: **responsum:** Remember not mine offences nor the offences of my forefathers, neither take thou vengeance of my sins; for thou, O Lord, art a Redeemer unto all that hope in thee. **versus:** For all thy judgements are just, and all thy ways are mercy and truth; and now, O Lord, remember me.
  - `liber-tobiae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** In all seasons bless God, and ask of Him to order thy goings; **responsum:** And in all seasons let thy counsels be steadfastly in Him. **versus:** Seek faithfully and with all thy strength to do such things as please Him.
  - `liber-tobiae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** My son, remember that we have but a frail life. **responsum:** If thou fear God thou shalt have great goods. **versus:** Be mindful of Him, and beware lest ever thou transgress His commandments.
  - `liber-tobiae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Our poverty was enough for us, that it might have been accounted riches. O that the money had never been, for which thou hast sent away our son, **responsum:** The staff of our old age. **versus:** Alas, my son, wherefore have we sent thee wandering, even thee, the light of our eyes?
  - `liber-tobiae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Bless the God of heaven, and confess Him before all living: **responsum:** For He hath had mercy upon you. **versus:** Bless Him, and sing praises unto Him, and tell of all His marvelous works.
  - `liber-tobiae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** It is time for me to return unto Him That sent me; **responsum:** But bless ye God, and tell of all His marvelous works. **versus:** Confess Him before all living, for He hath had mercy upon you.
  - `liber-job, feria, feria-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** O that my sins, whereby I have deserved wrath; **responsum:** And the calamity whereunder I suffer, were laid in the balances together. **versus:** For now it would appear heavier than the sand of the sea, therefore also my words are full of sorrow.
- `proprium-de-tempore/in-festo-epiphaniae/epiphania.json`
  - `epiphania, festum, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** This day, when the Lord was baptized in Jordan, the heavens were opened, and the Spirit descended like a dove, and abode upon Him, and, lo, the voice of the Father was heard, like unto thunder, saying: **responsum:** This is My beloved Son, in Whom I am well pleased. **versus:** The Holy Ghost descended in a bodily shape like a dove upon Him, and a voice came from heaven
  - `epiphania, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** There are three precious gifts which the wise men offered unto the Lord on this day, and they speak a mystery of the things of God: **responsum:** Gold, to show His kingly power; frankincense, for our Great High Priest; and myrrh, against the Lord's burying. **versus:** The wise men worshipped the Captain of our Salvation, as He lay in the manger, and when they had opened their treasures, they presented unto Him mystic gifts.
  - `epiphania, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** The Holy Ghost appeared in a bodily shape like a dove, and the voice of the Father was heard: **responsum:** This is My beloved Son, in Whom I am well pleased. **versus:** The heavens were opened unto him, and, lo, the voice of the Father was heard, like unto thunder, saying:
  - `epiphania, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** The kings of Tarshish and of the isles shall bring presents. **responsum:** The kings of Arabia and Saba shall offer gifts unto the Lord God. **versus:** All they from Saba shall come, they shall bring gold and incense.
  - `epiphania, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Shine, shine, O Jerusalem, for thy light is come; **responsum:** And the glory of the Lord is risen upon thee. **versus:** And the Gentiles shall walk in thy light, and kings in the brightness of thy rising.
  - `epiphania, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** All they from Saba shall come, they shall bring gold and incense, and they shall show forth the praises of the Lord. **responsum:** Alleluia, Alleluia, Alleluia. **versus:** The kings of Tarshish and of the isles shall bring presents, the kings of Arabia and Saba shall offer gifts.
  - `epiphania, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** There came wise men from the east to Jerusalem, asking and saying: Where is He That is born King of the Jews? for we have seen His star in the east; **responsum:** And are come to worship the Lord. **versus:** We have seen His star in the east.
  - `epiphania, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** The star which the wise men had seen in the East, went before them, till they came where the young Child was. **responsum:** And when they saw the star, they rejoiced with exceeding great joy. **versus:** And when they were come into the house, they found the young Child with Mary His Mother, and fell down and worshipped Him.
- `proprium-de-tempore/in-tempore-adventus/hebdomada-i-adventus.json`
  - `hebdomada-i-adventus, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** In the vision during the night I saw the Son of man coming on the clouds of heaven; and He was given kingship and glory. **responsum:** Nations and people of every language serve Him. **versus:** His dominion is an everlasting dominion that shall not be taken away, His kingship shall not be destroyed.
  - `hebdomada-i-adventus, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** We look for the Saviour, the Lord Jesus Christ; **responsum:** Who shall change our vile body, that it may be fashioned like unto His glorious Body. **versus:** We should live soberly, and righteously, and godly in this present world, looking for that blessed hope, and the glorious appearing of the great God.
  - `hebdomada-i-adventus, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Behold, the Virgin shall conceive, and bear a son, saith the Lord; **responsum:** And His name shall be called Wonderful, the Mighty God. (Alleluia.) **versus:** He shall sit upon the throne of David, and upon his kingdom for ever.
  - `hebdomada-i-adventus, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Hear the word of the Lord, O ye nations, and declare it in the ends of the earth; **responsum:** And in the isles afar off, and say: Our Saviour shall come. **versus:** Declare it and make it known, lift up your voice and cry aloud.
- `proprium-de-tempore/in-tempore-adventus/hebdomada-ii-adventus.json`
  - `hebdomada-ii-adventus, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Thy salvation cometh quickly, O Jerusalem; why art thou wasted with sorrow? Is there no counselor in thee, that pangs have taken thee? **responsum:** Fear not, for I will save thee and deliver thee. **versus:** For I am the Lord, thy God, the Holy One of Israel, thy Saviour.
  - `hebdomada-ii-adventus, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Behold, the Lord shall come, and all His saints with Him, and it shall come to pass in that day that the light shall be great; and they shall go out from Jerusalem like clean water; and the Lord shall be King for ever, **responsum:** Over all the earth. **versus:** Behold, the Lord cometh with an host, and in His hand are the kingdom, and power, and dominion.
  - `hebdomada-ii-adventus, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** O, thou city of Jerusalem, weep not, for the Lord hath repented Him concerning thee. **responsum:** And He will take away from thee all distress. **versus:** Behold, the Lord shall come with might, and His arm shall rule.
  - `hebdomada-ii-adventus, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Behold, there cometh the Lord, our defender, the Holy One of Israel, **responsum:** Wearing a royal crown upon His head. **versus:** And His dominion shall be from sea even to sea, and from the river even to the ends of the earth.
  - `hebdomada-ii-adventus, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** As a mother comforteth her children, so will I comfort you, saith the Lord; My help also cometh unto you out of Jerusalem, the city which I have chosen. **responsum:** And when ye see this, your heart shall rejoice. **versus:** I will place salvation in Zion and in Jerusalem My glory.
  - `hebdomada-ii-adventus, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Thou shalt yet plant vines upon thy mountains, O Jerusalem thou shalt sing for joy, for the day of the Lord cometh; arise, O Zion, and turn unto the Lord thy God; rejoice and be glad, O Jacob. **responsum:** For thy Saviour cometh from the midst of the nations. **versus:** Sing aloud for joy, O daughter of Zion; shout with gladness, O daughter of Jerusalem.
  - `hebdomada-ii-adventus, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** The Lord shall go forth out of Samaria unto the gate that looketh toward the East; and He shall come into Bethlehem, walking upon the waters of the redemption of Judah. **responsum:** Then shall every one be saved for, behold, He cometh. **versus:** And in mercy shall His throne be established, and He shall sit upon it in truth.
  - `hebdomada-ii-adventus, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Make haste, O Lord, make no tarrying. **responsum:** And deliver thy people. **versus:** O Lord, come and make no tarrying loose the bonds of thy people.
  - `hebdomada-ii-adventus, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Behold, the Lord cometh down with glory, and His host is with Him. **responsum:** To visit His people in peace, and to establish them in life everlasting. **versus:** Behold, our Lord cometh with an host.
- `proprium-de-tempore/in-tempore-adventus/hebdomada-iii-adventus.json`
  - `hebdomada-iii-adventus, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** O Lord, come, and make no tarrying loosen the bonds of thy people. **responsum:** And gather together into their own land them that are scattered abroad. **versus:** Stir up, O Lord, thy power, and come among us, to save us.
  - `hebdomada-iii-adventus, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Behold, there shall be a root of Jesse, which shall come for salvation unto the people, to it shall the Gentiles seek; **responsum:** And His name shall be glorious. **versus:** The Lord God shall give unto Him the throne of His father David, and He shall reign over the house of Jacob for ever.
  - `hebdomada-iii-adventus, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** The Lord will teach us of His ways, and we will walk in His paths. **responsum:** For out of Zion shall go forth the law, and the word of the Lord from Jerusalem. **versus:** Come ye, and let us go up to the mountain of the Lord, and to the house of the God of Jacob.
  - `hebdomada-iii-adventus, feria, feria-v, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** The Lord shall go forth and fight against the nations. **responsum:** And His feet shall stand upon the mount of Olives on the east. **versus:** And it shall be exalted above the hills, and all nations shall flow unto it.
  - `hebdomada-iii-adventus, feria, feria-v, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** The Fore-runner is for us entered, even the Lamb without spot **responsum:** Made an High Priest for ever after the order of Melchisedek. **versus:** This is that King of Righteousness without descent, nor end of life.
  - `hebdomada-iii-adventus, feria, feria-v, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** The Gentiles shall see thy Righteous One, and all kings thy Glorious one. **responsum:** And thou shalt be called by a new name, which the mouth of the Lord hath named. **versus:** Thou shalt also be a crown of glory in the hand of the Lord, and a royal diadem in the hand of thy God.
  - `hebdomada-iii-adventus, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Behold, the Lord shall appear upon a white cloud, **responsum:** And ten thousand of His saints with Him; and He shall have on His vesture, and on His thigh a name written King of kings, and Lord of lords. **versus:** He shall appear and not lie; though He tarry, wait for Him, because He will surely come.
  - `hebdomada-iii-adventus, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Thou, Bethlehem, art the city of the Most High God, out of thee shall He come forth That is to be Ruler in Israel; Whose goings forth have been from of old, from everlasting, and now shall He be great unto the ends of the earth. **responsum:** And this Man shall be the peace in our land, when He shall come. **versus:** He shall speak peace unto the Gentiles, and shall have dominion from sea to sea.
  - `hebdomada-iii-adventus, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** He That shall come, will come, and will not tarry; and there shall no more be fear in our borders. **responsum:** For He is our Saviour. **versus:** He shall tread down all our iniquities, and cast all our sins into the depths of the sea.
  - `hebdomada-iii-adventus, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Weep not, O Egypt, for the Ruler cometh unto thee, and the depths shall be moved at His presence. **responsum:** To deliver His people out of the hand of the mighty. **versus:** Behold, the Lord of hosts, thy God, cometh with great power.
  - `hebdomada-iii-adventus, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Her time is near to come, and her days shall not be prolonged. **responsum:** Lord shall have mercy on Jacob, and Israel shall be saved. **versus:** Turn again, O Virgin of Israel, turn again to thy cities.
  - `hebdomada-iii-adventus, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** The Lord shall come down like rain upon a fleece. **responsum:** In His days shall righteousness flourish, and abundance of peace. **versus:** All the kings of the earth shall fall down before Him, all nations shall serve Him.
- `proprium-de-tempore/in-tempore-adventus/hebdomada-iv-adventus.json`
  - `hebdomada-iv-adventus, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Blow ye the trumpet in Zion, call together the nations, tell it out among the people, and say: **responsum:** Behold, God our Saviour cometh. **versus:** Tell it out and make it to be heard; speak aloud and cry.
  - `hebdomada-iv-adventus, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** The sceptre shall not depart from Judah, nor the law-giver from his loins, until he that shall be sent cometh. **responsum:** And unto him shall the longing of the Gentiles be. **versus:** His eyes shall be bright with wine, and his teeth white with milk.
  - `hebdomada-iv-adventus, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** I must decrease, but He must increase; He it is Who, coming after me is preferred before me; **responsum:** Whose shoe's latchet I am not worthy to unloose. **versus:** I baptize you with water; but He shall baptize you with the Holy Ghost.
  - `hebdomada-iv-adventus, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Unto us shall a Child be born, and His name shall be called the Mighty God. **responsum:** He shall sit upon the throne of His father David, and shall reign, and the government shall be upon His shoulder. **versus:** In Him shall all the kindreds of the earth be blessed; all nations shall serve Him.
  - `hebdomada-iv-adventus, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Behold, the fullness of the time is come, wherein God hath sent forth His Son into the world, born of a Virgin, made under the law; **responsum:** To redeem them that were under the law. **versus:** God, for His great love wherewith He loved us, hath sent His own Son in the likeness of sinful flesh.
  - `hebdomada-iv-adventus, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** I have sworn, saith the Lord, that I will not be wroth any more with the earth; for the mountains and the hills shall receive My righteousness. **responsum:** And the covenant of My peace shall be in Jerusalem. **versus:** My salvation is near to come, and My righteousness to be revealed.
  - `hebdomada-iv-adventus, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** We will not go back from thee. Thou, O Lord, shalt quicken us, and we will call upon thy name. **responsum:** Cause thy face to shine upon us, and we shall be saved. **versus:** Remember us, O Lord, with the favour that Thou showest unto thy people; O visit us with thy salvation.
- `proprium-de-tempore/in-tempore-adventus/in-tempore-adventus.json`
  - `hebdomada-i-adventus, feria, feria-iii, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Behold, I, the Lord your God, come from the South **responsum:** To visit you in peace. **versus:** I will look again upon you and make you to increase ye shall be multiplied, and I will establish My covenant with you.
  - `hebdomada-i-adventus, feria, feria-ii, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Receive, O Virgin Mary, receive the word of the Lord, which is sent thee by His Angel; thou shalt conceive, and shalt bring forth God and Man together. **responsum:** And thou shalt be called blessed among all women. (Alleluia.) **versus:** Thou shalt bring forth a son, and remain a maiden undefiled; thou shalt conceive and be a Mother, still Virgin unspotted.
  - `hebdomada-i-adventus, feria, feria-ii, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Sing, O heavens; and be joyful, O earth; and break forth into singing, O mountains, for our Lord will come; **responsum:** And will have mercy on His afflicted. **versus:** In His days shall righteousness flourish and abundance of peace.
  - `hebdomada-i-adventus, feria, feria-ii, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** There shall no strangers pass through Jerusalem any more **responsum:** For in that day the mountains shall drop down sweet wine, and the hills shall flow with milk and honey, saith the Lord. **versus:** God shall come from Lebanon, and the Holy One from the thick and shady mountain.
  - `hebdomada-i-adventus, feria, feria-iii, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** O ye mountains of Israel, shoot forth your branches and blossom and bring forth fruit. **responsum:** The day of the Lord is at hand to come. **versus:** Drop down, ye heavens, from above, and let the skies pour down the Righteous One; let the earth open, and let her bring forth the Saviour.
  - `hebdomada-i-adventus, feria, feria-iii, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Let the mountains break forth into singing, and the hills bring forth righteousness **responsum:** For the Lord, the Light of the world, cometh with power. **versus:** Out of Zion shall go forth the law, and the word of the Lord from Jerusalem.
  - `hebdomada-i-adventus, feria, feria-iv, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Christ our King cometh. **responsum:** And John hath testified of Him, that He is the Lamb that should come! **versus:** The kings shall shut their mouths at Him, all nations shall serve Him.
  - `hebdomada-i-adventus, feria, feria-iv, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Of a long time, said Ezekiel the Prophet, I saw the gate shut behold, God went forth from it before the ages for the salvation of the world. **responsum:** And it was shut again, for it is a figure of the Virgin, in that after child-birth she remained a Virgin still. **versus:** The Lord alone shall enter by the gate that thou savest.
- `proprium-de-tempore/in-tempore-adventus/quatuor-tempora-adventus.json`
  - `quatuor-tempora-adventus, feria-iv, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** O thou that bringest good tidings of peace to Jerusalem, lift up thy voice with strength! **responsum:** Say unto the cities of Judah, and to the inhabitants of Jerusalem: Behold, our God will come, for Whom we waited. **versus:** O thou that tellest good tidings to Zion, get thee up into the high mountain, lift up thy voice with strength.
  - `quatuor-tempora-adventus, feria-iv, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** There shall come a Star out of Jacob, and a Man shall rise out of Israel, and shall smite through all the princes of the aliens. **responsum:** And all the earth shall be His possession. **versus:** All kings shall fall down before Him, all nations shall serve Him.
  - `quatuor-tempora-adventus, feria-iv, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** The Lord, the Ruler, cometh quickly. **responsum:** And His name shall be called Emmanuel. **versus:** In His days shall righteousness flourish, and abundance of peace.
  - `quatuor-tempora-adventus, feria-vi, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Send forth the Lamb, O Lord, the Ruler of the land; **responsum:** From the rock in the wilderness unto the mount of the daughter of Zion. **versus:** Show us thy mercy, O Lord, and grant us thy salvation.
  - `quatuor-tempora-adventus, feria-vi, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Drop down, ye heavens, from above, and let the skies pour down the Righteous One. **responsum:** Let the earth open, and let her bring forth the Saviour. **versus:** Send forth the Lamb, O Lord, the Ruler of the land, from the rock in the wilderness unto the mount of the daughter of Zion.
  - `quatuor-tempora-adventus, feria-vi, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** The waste places have brought forth sweet-smelling buds for Israel; for, behold, our God will come with power. **responsum:** And His brightness is with Him. **versus:** Out of Zion the perfection of beauty, our God shall come manifestly.
  - `quatuor-tempora-adventus, sabbatum, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** There shall come forth a rod out of the stem of Jesse, and a Flower shall grow out of his roots. **responsum:** And righteousness shall be the girdle of his loins, and faithfulness the girdle of his reins. (Alleluia.) **versus:** And the Spirit of the Lord shall rest upon him, the Spirit of wisdom and understanding, the Spirit of counsel and might.
  - `quatuor-tempora-adventus, sabbatum, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Behold, the root of Jesse that shall arise to bring forth judgment to the Gentiles, in him shall the Gentiles trust. **responsum:** And his name shall be blessed for ever. **versus:** The Kings shall shut their mouths at him, to him shall the Gentiles seek.
- `proprium-de-tempore/in-tempore-adventus/vigilia-nativitatis.json`
  - `vigilia-nativitatis, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Sanctify yourselves today, and be ready; for on the morrow, ye shall see **responsum:** The majesty of God upon you. **versus:** This day ye shall know that the Lord cometh, and in the morning, then ye shall see.
  - `vigilia-nativitatis, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Stand still, and ye shall see the help of the Lord with you; O Judah and Jerusalem, fear not. **responsum:** Tomorrow ye shall go out, and the Lord will be with you. **versus:** Sanctify yourselves, O ye children of Israel, and be ready.
  - `vigilia-nativitatis, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Sanctify yourselves, O ye children of Israel, saith the Lord, for on the morrow the Lord will come down. **responsum:** And will take away from you all sickness. **versus:** On the morrow the sins of the earth shall be washed away, and the Saviour of the world will be our King.
- `proprium-de-tempore/in-tempore-nativitatis/circumcisio.json`
  - `circumcisio, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Behold the Lamb of God, behold Him Which taketh away the sins of the world; behold Him of Whom I said unto you: He That cometh after me is preferred before me **responsum:** Whose shoe's latchet I am not worthy to unloose. **versus:** He that is of the earth speaketh of the earth; He That cometh from heaven is above all.
  - `circumcisio, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** This day which is breaking is holy; O come, ye Gentiles, and worship the Lord. **responsum:** For this day is much light come down unto us from heaven. **versus:** This is the day which the Lord hath made, let us rejoice and be glad in it.
  - `circumcisio, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Blessed be He That cometh in the name of the Lord! God is the Lord Who hath showed us light. **responsum:** Alleluia, Alleluia. **versus:** This is the day which the Lord hath made, let us rejoice and be glad in it.
  - `circumcisio, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** The heart of the Virgin was fixed, when the Angel declared unto her the mystery of God, and she conceived, then did she receive in her pure womb Him That is fairer than the children of men. **responsum:** And, she that is blessed for ever, brought forth for us God and man. **versus:** Soon rises, in that modest shrine, The Temple of the Lord Divine The stainless and unwedded one, Within her womb conceived the Son.
  - `circumcisio, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Blessed and worshipful art thou, O Virgin Mary; from thee, still maiden undefiled, the Saviour came a little Child. **responsum:** He Whose glory filled the heavens lay in a manger. **versus:** O Lord, I have heard thy speech and was afraid; I considered thy works and trembled. O Thou That dwellest between the two living creatures!
  - `circumcisio, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** The Virgin-Mother that knew not a man, bore, but travailed not. **responsum:** She fed the Saviour of the world, The King of Angel hosts above, Jesus, our Redeemer blest, From the fountain of her breast. **versus:** Soon rises in that modest shrine, The Temple of the Lord Divine; The stainless and unwedded one, Within her womb conceived the Son.
- `proprium-de-tempore/in-tempore-nativitatis/innocentes.json`
  - `innocentes, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** A hundred forty four thousand, which were redeemed from the earth; these are they which were not defiled with women. **responsum:** For they remained virgins; therefore are they kings before God, and the Lamb of God is with them. **versus:** These are they which came out of great tribulation, and have washed their robes in the Blood of the Lamb.
  - `innocentes, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** I heard under the altar the voices of them that were slain, saying: **responsum:** How long dost Thou not avenge our blood? And it was said unto them from God: Rest yet for a little season, until the number of your brethren be fulfilled. **versus:** I saw under the altar of God the souls of them that were slain for the Word of God, and for the testimony which they held, and they cried with a loud voice, saying:
  - `innocentes, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** They worshipped Him That liveth for ever and ever. **responsum:** And cast their crowns before the throne of the Lord their God. **versus:** And they fell down upon their faces before the throne, and blessed Him That liveth for ever and ever.
  - `innocentes, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** The blood of thy saints have they shed like water round about Jerusalem. **responsum:** And there was none to bury them. **versus:** The dead bodies of thy servants have they given to be meat unto the fowls of the air, the flesh of thy saints unto the beasts of the earth.
  - `innocentes, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** These holy ones suffered for thy sake, O Lord take vengeance for them. **responsum:** For day by day they cry unto thee. **versus:** Avenge, O Lord, the blood of thy saints which is shed.
  - `innocentes, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** These are they which have not defiled their garments. **responsum:** They shall walk with Me in white, for they are worthy. **versus:** These are they which were not defiled with women for they are virgins.
  - `innocentes, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** The saints sung a new song before the throne of God and the Lamb. **responsum:** And their voices were echoed on earth. **versus:** These were redeemed from among men, being the first-fruits unto God, and to the Lamb, and in their mouth was found no guile.
  - `innocentes, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** I saw under the Altar of God the souls of them that were slain for the word of God, which they held, and they cried with a loud voice **responsum:** Avenge, O Lord, the blood of thy saints, which is shed. **versus:** Under the throne of God all the saints cry aloud
  - `innocentes, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** What are these which are arrayed in white robes? and whence came they? And he said to me: **responsum:** These are they which came out of great tribulation, and have washed their robes, and made them white in the Blood of the Lamb. **versus:** I saw under the Altar of God the souls of them which were slain for the word of God, and for the testimony which they held.
- `proprium-de-tempore/in-tempore-nativitatis/joannes-apostolus.json`
  - `joannes-apostolus, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Right worthy of honour is the blessed Apostle John, who leaned on the Lord's bosom at the Last Supper. **responsum:** To Him did Christ upon the Cross commit His mother, virgin to virgin. **versus:** The Lord chose him for his clean maidenhood, and loved him more than all the rest.
  - `joannes-apostolus, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** This is the disciple which testifieth of these things, and wrote these things. **responsum:** And we know that his testimony is true. **versus:** He drank in the rivers of the Gospel from the Lord's Breast as from an holy fountain.
  - `joannes-apostolus, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** This is that most blessed Evangelist and Apostle John. **responsum:** Who was found worthy that the Lord should honour him more than all the rest, by a special privilege of love. **versus:** This is the disciple whom Jesus loved, which also leaned on the Lord's Breast at supper.
  - `joannes-apostolus, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Him that overcometh will I make a pillar in My temple, saith the Lord, **responsum:** And I will write My name upon him, and the name of the city, which is New Jerusalem. **versus:** To him that overcometh will I give to eat of the tree of life, which is in the midst of the Paradise of My God.
  - `joannes-apostolus, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Jesus loved him, because his singular gift of purity made him more worthy of love. **responsum:** He chose him for a virgin unto Himself, and he remaineth a virgin for ever. **versus:** At the end, when He was dying upon the Cross, to him did He commit His mother, maiden to maiden.
  - `joannes-apostolus, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** In the midst of the congregation did the Lord open his mouth. **responsum:** And filled him with the spirit of wisdom and understanding. **versus:** He made him rich with joy and gladness.
  - `joannes-apostolus, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** In that day will I take thee, O My servant, and will make thee as a signet before Me. **responsum:** For I have chosen thee, saith the Lord. **versus:** Be thou faithful unto death, and I will give thee a crown of life.
  - `joannes-apostolus, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** This is that John which leaned on the Lord's Breast at supper **responsum:** Even that blessed Apostle unto whom were made known the secret things of heaven. **versus:** He drank in the rivers of the Gospel from the Lord's Breast, as from an holy fountain.
- `proprium-de-tempore/in-tempore-nativitatis/nativitas.json`
  - `nativitas, infra-octavam, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** How is the King of heaven attended? He that containeth the world is laid in a stable; **responsum:** Lying in a manger, reigning in heaven. **versus:** Unto us is born, this day, in the city of David, a Saviour, Which is Christ the Lord.
  - `nativitas, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** This is the day whereon the King of heaven was pleased to be born of a Virgin, that He might bring back to heaven man who was lost. **responsum:** There is joy among the hosts of Angels, because eternal salvation hath appeared unto men. **versus:** Glory to God in the highest, and on earth peace, to men of goodwill.
  - `nativitas, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** This day is the true peace come down unto us from heaven. **responsum:** This day throughout the whole world the skies drop down sweetness. **versus:** This day is the daybreak of our new redemption, of the restoring of the old, of everlasting joy.
  - `nativitas, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** O ye shepherds, speak, and tell us what ye have seen; who is appeared in the earth? **responsum:** We saw the new-born Child, and Angels singing praise to the Lord. **versus:** Speak; what have ye seen? And tell us of the Birth of Christ.
  - `nativitas, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** How great is this mystery, how wonderful is the teaching of the faith! The beasts saw the new-born Lord lying in a manger. **responsum:** Blessed is that Virgin whose womb was made meet to bear our Lord Christ. **versus:** Hail, Mary, full of grace the Lord is with thee.
  - `nativitas, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Blessed is God's holy Mother, Mary, maiden undefiled. **responsum:** This day hath she brought forth the Saviour of the world. **versus:** Blessed is she that believed; for there is a performance of all those things which were told her from the Lord.
  - `nativitas, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** O how holy and how spotless is thy virginity; I am too dull to praise thee: **responsum:** For thou hast borne in thy breast Him Whom the heavens cannot contain. **versus:** Blessed art thou among women, and blessed is the fruit of thy womb.
  - `nativitas, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Blessed be the womb of the Virgin Mary, which bore the Son of the Eternal Father, and blessed be the paps which give suck to Christ our Lord. **responsum:** This day hath He been pleased for the salvation of the world to be born of a Virgin. **versus:** This day which is breaking is holy; O come, ye Gentiles, and worship the Lord.
  - `nativitas, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** The Word was made flesh, and dwelt among us. **responsum:** And we beheld His glory, the glory as of the Only-begotten of the Father, full of grace and truth. **versus:** All things were made by Him, and without Him was not anything made.
- `proprium-de-tempore/in-tempore-nativitatis/stephanus.json`
  - `stephanus, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** And Stephen, full of grace and power **responsum:** Did great wonders and miracles among the people. **versus:** There arose certain of the synagogue, disputing with Stephen; and they were not able to resist the wisdom, and the Spirit which spoke.
  - `stephanus, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** All that sat in the council, looking steadfastly on Stephen, saw **responsum:** His face as it had been the face of an angel standing among them. **versus:** Full of grace and power, he did great wonders and miracles among the people.
  - `stephanus, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** The blessed Stephen looked up steadfastly into heaven, and saw the glory of God, and said **responsum:** Behold, I see the heavens opened, and the Son of Man standing at the right hand of the power of God. **versus:** But Stephen, being full of the Holy Ghost, looked up steadfastly into heaven, and saw the glory of God, and said
  - `stephanus, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** They stoned Stephen, calling upon God and saying: **responsum:** Lord Jesus Christ, receive my spirit; and lay not this sin to their charge. **versus:** And he kneeled down, and cried with a loud voice, saying
  - `stephanus, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** They ran upon him with one accord, and cast him out of the city, calling upon God, and saying: **responsum:** Lord Jesus, receive my spirit. **versus:** And the witnesses laid down their clothes at a young man's feet, whose name was Saul; and they stoned Stephen, calling upon God, and saying
  - `stephanus, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** The ungodly fell upon the righteous, to put him to death. **responsum:** But he received the stones with joy, that he might earn a crown of glory. **versus:** They stopped their ears, and ran upon him with one accord.
  - `stephanus, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Stephen, the servant of God, who was stoned by the Jews, saw the heavens opened he saw and entered in. **responsum:** Blessed is he, unto whom the heavens were opened. **versus:** While his poor body was crushed by the hurtling shower of stones, God's brightness broke upon him out of the heavenly palaces.
  - `stephanus, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** The gates of heaven were opened to Christ's blessed martyr Stephen, and he is the first of all the martyrs. **responsum:** Wherefore he reigneth crowned in heaven. **versus:** For he was the first to make an offering of his death to that Saviour Who vouchsafed to suffer death for us.
- `proprium-de-tempore/in-tempore-paschali/ascensio.json`
  - `ascensio, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Being seen of them forty days after that He had suffered, and speaking of the kingdom of God. Alleluia. **responsum:** And while they beheld, He was taken up, and a cloud received Him out of their sight. Alleluia. **versus:** And, eating together with them, He commanded them that they should not depart from Jerusalem, but wait for the Promise of the Father.
  - `ascensio, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** The Lord hath set His beauty above the stars; **responsum:** His loveliness is in the clouds of heaven, and His Name endureth for ever. Alleluia. **versus:** His going forth is from the end of the heaven, and His circuit unto the ends of it.
  - `ascensio, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Be Thou exalted, O Lord. Alleluia. **responsum:** In thine Own strength. Alleluia. **versus:** O God, Thou hast set thy glory above the heavens.
  - `ascensio, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** My time is come that I should return unto Him That sent Me, saith the Lord. Be not sorrowful, neither let your heart be troubled. **responsum:** I pray the Father for you, that He may keep you. Alleluia, Alleluia. **versus:** If I go not away, the Comforter will not come unto you; where I am ascended, I will send Him unto you.
  - `ascensio, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Let not your heart be troubled; I go unto the Father, and when I am taken from you, I will send unto you, alleluia, **responsum:** The Spirit of truth, and your heart shall rejoice. Alleluia. **versus:** I will pray the Father, and He shall give you another Comforter.
  - `ascensio, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** When Christ ascended up on high, He led captivity captive, **responsum:** He gave gifts unto men. Alleluia, Alleluia, Alleluia. **versus:** God is gone up with a shout, and the Lord with the sound of a trumpet.
  - `ascensio, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** I will pray the Father, and He shall give you another Comforter, **responsum:** That He may abide with you for ever, even the Spirit of truth. Alleluia. **versus:** For if I go not away, the Comforter will not come unto you; but if I depart, I will send Him unto you.
  - `ascensio, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Thou makest the clouds the chariot, O Lord, **responsum:** Thou walkest upon the wings of the wind. Alleluia. **versus:** Thou art clothed with honour and majesty, covering thyself with light as with a garment;
  - `ascensio, dominica-infra-octavam, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** For if I go not away, the Comforter will not come unto you; but if I depart, I will send Him unto you; **responsum:** And when He is come, He will guide you into all truth. Alleluia. **versus:** For He shall not speak of Himself; but whatsoever He shall hear, that shall He speak and He will show you things to come.

He is risen now: **responsum:** Come and see, alleluia, alleluia. **versus:** Seek ye Jesus of Nazareth, Which was crucified? He is risen, He is not here.
- `proprium-de-tempore/in-tempore-paschali/hebdomada-iii-paschae.json`
  - `hebdomada-iii-paschae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Coming from Lebanon how sweet she is, alleluia: **responsum:** And the sweet smell of her garments, above all aromatical spices, alleluia, alleluia. **versus:** Thy lips are as a dropping honeycomb, honey and milk are under thy tongue.
  - `hebdomada-iii-paschae, feria-ii, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** I saw the gate of the city, which looketh toward the East, and written thereon the names of the (Twelve) Apostles and of the Lamb. **responsum:** And upon the walls thereof a guard of Angels. alleluia. **versus:** I saw a new heaven and a new earth coming down out of heaven.
  - `hebdomada-iii-paschae, feria-ii, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** The Angel showed me the fountain of the water of life and he said unto me, Alleluia. **responsum:** Here worship God. Alleluia, Alleluia, Alleluia. **versus:** When I had heard and seen, I fell down to worship before the feet of the Angel, which showed me these things, and he said unto me
  - `hebdomada-iii-paschae, feria-iii, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** I saw Jerusalem coming down out of heaven, adorned with pure gold, and garnished with precious stones. **responsum:** Alleluia, Alleluia. **versus:** The building of the wall of it was of jasper and the city was pure gold, like unto clear glass.
  - `hebdomada-iii-paschae, feria-iii, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Upon the diadem of Aaron's head was graven the Majesty of the Lord, **responsum:** While as the work of God was in doing. Alleluia, Alleluia, Alleluia. **versus:** For in the long garment which he had, was the whole world, and in the four rows of the stones was the glory of the fathers graven.
  - `hebdomada-iii-paschae, feria-iv, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Thy streets, O Jerusalem, shall bel paved with pure gold, Alleluia, and the song of joy shall be sung in thee. Alleluia. **responsum:** And all that pass through all thy streets shall say Alleluia, Alleluia. **versus:** Thy light shall be exceeding glorious, and all the ends of the earth shall worship thee.
  - `hebdomada-iii-paschae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Thou art worthy, O Lord, to take the book, and to open the seals thereof; alleluia: because thou wast slain, and hast redeemed us to God, **responsum:** In thy blood, alleluia. **versus:** And hast made us to our God a kingdom and priests.

  - `hebdomada-passionis, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** How long shall mine enemy be exalted over me? **responsum:** Consider, and hear me, O Lord my God! **versus:** Those that trouble me will rejoice when I am moved but I have trusted in thy mercy.
  - `hebdomada-passionis, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Thou art my God; be not far from me, for trouble is near; **responsum:** For there is none to help. **versus:** But be not thy strength far from me; O Lord, haste thee to help me.

  - `dominica-in-palmis, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Give heed to me, O Lord, and hearken to the voice of them that contend with me. **responsum:** Shall evil be recompensed for good? for they have digged a pit for my soul. **versus:** Remember that I stood before thee to speak good for them, and to turn away thy wrath from them.
  - `dominica-in-palmis, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** The enemy hath enclosed my ways; he lay in wait for me as a lion; in secret places he hath filled me, and made me drunken with bitterness; they have cut off my life in the dungeon, and cast a stone upon me. **responsum:** O Lord, behold all their iniquity, and plead the cause of my soul, Thou That art the Redeemer of my life! **versus:** I was a derision to all my people, and their song all the day.
  - `dominica-in-palmis, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Save me, O God, for the waters are come in unto my soul; hide not thy face from me; **responsum:** For I am in trouble.   - `dominica-in-palmis, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** O Lord, be not Thou far from me; spare me in the day of evil, let them be confounded that persecute me; **responsum:** But let not me be confounded. **versus:** Let all mine enemies which seek after my soul be confounded.
  - `dominica-in-palmis, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** The Lord is with me as a Mighty Terrible One; therefore have they persecuted me, and have not been able to understand.
