  - `dominica-in-palmis, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** The ungodly said, reasoning with themselves, but not aright; Let us lie in wait for the righteous, because he is clean contrary to our doings he professeth to have the knowledge of God, he calleth himself the Son of God, and boasteth that he hath God to his Father. **responsum:** Let us see if his words be true; and, if he be indeed the Son of God, let Him deliver him from our hand; let us condemn him with a shameful death. **versus:** We are esteemed of him as counterfeits, and he abstaineth from our ways as from filthiness, and commendeth the end of the just.
  - `dominica-in-palmis, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Liars are come round about me, they have fallen upon me with scourges without a cause. **responsum:** But do Thou, O Lord my Redeemer, avenge me! **versus:** For trouble is near, and there is none to help.
  - `hebdomada-sancta, feria-ii, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** The ungodly said: Let us oppress the righteous man without cause, and swallow him up alive, as the grave; let us make his memorial to perish from the earth, and cast lots among us for his spoils and those murderers laid by store for themselves, but of evil. **responsum:** Fools and haters loathe wisdom, and are guilty in their thoughts. **versus:** Such things they did imagine, and were deceived, for their own wickedness blinded them.
  - `hebdomada-sancta, feria-ii, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** I became a reproach unto mine enemies they looked upon me and shaked their heads. **responsum:** Help me, O Lord my God! **versus:** They have spoken against me with a lying tongue they compassed me about also with words of hatred.
  - `hebdomada-sancta, feria-ii, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** False witnesses are risen up against me, and such as breathe out cruelty they have gone about to kill me, neither spared they to spit in my face; their spears have wounded me, and all my bones are out of joint. **responsum:** But as for me, I counted myself as one that is dead upon the earth. **versus:** They poured forth their fury upon me, they gnashed upon me with their teeth.
  - `hebdomada-sancta, feria-iii, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** I have suffered defaming and fear from them that were my familiars they watched for my halting, saying Let us entice him, and prevail against him. But Thou, O Lord, art with me, as a Mighty Terrible One. **responsum:** Let them stumble into everlasting confusion, that I may see thy vengeance upon them, for unto thee have I opened my cause. **versus:** O Lord, plead Thou the cause of my soul, Thou That art the Redeemer of my life.
  - `hebdomada-sancta, feria-iii, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** For thy sake, O God of Israel, I have borne reproach; shame hath covered my face; I am become a stranger unto my brethren, and an alien unto my mother's children. **responsum:** For the zeal of thine house hath eaten me up. **versus:** Draw nigh unto my soul, and redeem it; deliver me, because of mine enemies.
  - `hebdomada-sancta, feria-iii, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** The congregation of the people hath compassed me about, but I rewarded no evil unto him that rewarded evil unto me. **responsum:** O Lord, let the wickedness of the wicked come to an end, but establish the just. **versus:** Judge me, O Lord, according to my righteousness, and according to mine integrity that is in me.
  - `hebdomada-sancta, feria-iv, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** They have spoken against me with a lying tongue; they compassed me about also with words of hatred: in return for my love they were my adversaries: **responsum:** But I gave myself unto prayer; and Thou hast heard me, Lord my God! **versus:** And they have rewarded me evil for good, and hatred for my love.
  - `hebdomada-sancta, feria-iv, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** The ungodly said, reasoning with themselves, but not aright; Let us lie in wait for the righteous, because he is clean contrary to our doings he professeth to have the knowledge of God, he calleth himself the Son of God, and boasteth that he hath God to his Father. **responsum:** Let us see if his words be true; and, if he be indeed the Son of God, let Him deliver him from our hand; let us condemn him with a shameful death. **versus:** We are esteemed of him as counterfeits, and he abstaineth from our ways as from filthiness, and commendeth the end of the just.
  - `hebdomada-sancta, feria-iv, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Liars are come round about me, they have fallen upon me with scourges without a cause. **responsum:** But do Thou, O Lord my Redeemer, avenge me! **versus:** For trouble is near, and there is none to help.
- `proprium-de-tempore/in-tempore-passionis/triduum.json`
  - `coena-domini, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** At the Mount of Olives He prayed unto the Father: O My Father, if it be possible, let this cup pass from Me! **responsum:** The spirit indeed is willing, but the flesh is weak. **versus:** Watch and pray, that ye enter not into temptation.
  - `coena-domini, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** My Soul is exceeding sorrowful, even unto death: tarry ye here and watch with Me **responsum:** Yet a little while, and ye shall see the multitude close Me in. Ye shall flee; and I will go to be offered a sacrifice for you. **versus:** Behold, the hour is at hand, and the Son of man is betrayed into the hands of sinners.
  - `coena-domini, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Behold, when we shall see Him, He hath no form nor comeliness there is no beauty in Him this is He Which hath borne our griefs and carried our sorrows but He was wounded for our transgressions **responsum:** And with His stripes we are healed. **versus:** Surely He hath borne our griefs and carried our sorrows.
  - `coena-domini, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Mine own friend hath betrayed Me by the sign of a kiss: Whomsoever I shall kiss, That Same is He; hold Him fast. This was the traitorous sign which he gave, even he who murdered with a kiss. **responsum:** Woe unto that man! He cast down the price of blood, and went, and hanged himself. **versus:** It had been good for that man if he had not been born.
  - `coena-domini, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** The vile trader Judas came to the Lord to kiss Him, and He, as a guileless Lamb, refused not a kiss to Judas, **responsum:** Who, for a certain number of pence, betrayed Christ to the Jews. **versus:** It had been good for that man if he had not been born.
  - `coena-domini, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** One of My disciples shall betray Me this night. Woe unto that man by whom I am betrayed! **responsum:** It had been good for that man if he had not been born. **versus:** He that dippeth his hand with Me in the dish, the same shall betray Me into the hands of sinners.
  - `coena-domini, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** I was like a gentle lamb that is brought to the slaughter, and I knew not that mine enemies had devised devices against me, saying: **responsum:** Come, let us put (poison of a deadly) tree into his bread, and let us cut him off from the land of the living. **versus:** All they that hate me devised my hurt against me: they plotted together to do me evil, saying:
  - `coena-domini, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Could ye not watch with Me one hour, ye that called one on the other to die for Me? **responsum:** Or see ye not Judas, how that he sleepeth not, but maketh haste to betray Me to the Jews? **versus:** Why sleep ye? Rise, and pray, lest ye enter into temptation.
  - `coena-domini, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** The elders of the people consulted **responsum:** That they might take Jesus by subtilty, and kill Him they came out, as against a thief, with swords and staves. **versus:** The chief Priests and the Pharisees gathered a council.
  - `parasceve, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** All my friends have forsaken me, and mine enemies have prevailed against me; he whom I loved hath betrayed me. **responsum:** Mine enemy sharpeneth his eyes upon me; he breaketh me with breach upon breach: and in my thirst they gave me vinegar to drink. **versus:** I am numbered with the transgressors; and my life is not spared.
  - `parasceve, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** The veil of the Temple was rent in twain, from the top to the bottom, **responsum:** And all the earth did quake: the thief on the cross cried, saying: Lord, remember me when Thou comest into thy kingdom! **versus:** The rocks rent, and the graves were opened, and many bodies of the saints, which slept, arose.
  - `parasceve, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** I had planted thee a noble vineyard; **responsum:** How then art thou turned into a degenerate plant, which willest that Barabbas should be released unto thee, and that I should be crucified. **versus:** I fenced thee, and gathered out the stones from thee, and built a tower in the midst of the land.
  - `sabbatum-sanctum, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** He hath been brought as a lamb to the slaughter, and while he was evil entreated he opened not his mouth: he was delivered up to death **responsum:** That he might quicken his people. **versus:** He hath poured out his soul unto death, and he was numbered with the transgressors.
  - `sabbatum-sanctum, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Arise, O Jerusalem, and put off thy garments of rejoicing: cover thee with sackcloth and ashes **responsum:** For the Saviour of Israel hath been slain in the midst of thee. **versus:** Let thy tears run down like a river day and night, and let not the apple of thine eye cease.
  - `sabbatum-sanctum, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** O my people! lament, like a virgin girded with sack-cloth for the husband of her youth, howl, ye shepherds, in sack-cloth and ashes **responsum:** For the day of the Lord is at hand, and it is great and very terrible. **versus:** Gird yourselves, ye Priests, and howl, ye ministers of the altar: cast up ashes upon you.
  - `parasceve, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Are ye come out, as against a thief, with swords and staves, for to take Me? **responsum:** I sat daily with you, teaching in the Temple, and ye laid no hold on Me; and, now when ye have scourged Me, ye lead Me away to crucify Me! **versus:** And when they had laid hands on Jesus, and taken Him, He said unto them:
  - `parasceve, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** The Jews crucified Jesus: and there was darkness (over all the land, unto the ninth hour): and about the ninth hour Jesus cried with a loud voice, (saying): My God, (My God,) why hast Thou forsaken Me? **responsum:** And He bowed His Head, and gave up the Ghost. **versus:** When Jesus had cried with a loud voice, He said: Father, into thy hands I commend My Spirit.
  - `parasceve, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** I have given the dearly-beloved of My soul into the hand of her enemies and Mine heritage is become unto Me as a lion in the forest; the enemy crieth out against Me, saying: Assemble yourselves together, hasten to devour Him: they have made My portion a desolate wilderness, and the whole land mourneth unto Me: **responsum:** Because there is none found that will know Me, nor do well. **versus:** There be risen up against me such as breathe out cruelty, and they have not spared my soul.
  - `sabbatum-sanctum, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Our Shepherd, even the Fountain of living waters, is gone from us; He passed away, and the sun was darkened. **responsum:** For now hath our Saviour bound him captive, which bound the first man captive; this day hath He burst the gates and bars of death. **versus:** The bands of hell He hath utterly abolished, and hath done away the power of the devil.
  - `sabbatum-sanctum, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** O all ye that pass by, behold and see; **responsum:** If there be any sorrow like unto my sorrow. **versus:** O all ye nations, behold, and see my sorrow,
  - `sabbatum-sanctum, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Behold how the righteous dieth, and no man taketh it to heart; and the just are taken away, and none considereth. From the midst of sinners is the righteous translated; **responsum:** And his memory is in peace. **versus:** As a lamb before his shearers is dumb, so He opened not His mouth; He was taken from prison and from judgment.
  - `parasceve, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** They have turned me over into the hands of the wicked: they also have numbered me with the transgressors, neither have they spared my life: the mighty are gathered together against me; **responsum:** And stand up against me like giants. **versus:** Strangers are risen up against me, and oppressors seek after my soul.
  - `parasceve, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** That wicked one betrayed Jesus to the chief-priests and elders of the people **responsum:** But Peter followed Him afar off, to see the end. **versus:** And they led Him away to Caiaphas the High Priest, where the Scribes and Pharisees were assembled.
  - `parasceve, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Mine eyes do fail with tears, because the Comforter that should relieve me is far from me. Behold, O all ye nations **responsum:** If there be any sorrow like unto my sorrow. **versus:** O all ye that pass by, behold, and see
  - `sabbatum-sanctum, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** The kings of the earth set themselves, and the rulers take counsel together **responsum:** Against the Lord, and against His Anointed. **versus:** Why do the heathen rage? and the people imagine a vain thing,
  - `sabbatum-sanctum, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** I am counted with them that go down into the pit. **responsum:** I am as a man that hath no strength, lying nerveless among the dead. **versus:** They have laid me in the lowest pit, in darkness, and in the shadow of death.
  - `sabbatum-sanctum, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** After that the Lord was buried, they sealed the sepulchre, rolling a stone to the door of the sepulchre **responsum:** Setting a watch to keep Him. **versus:** The chief priests came together unto Pilate, and made that request unto him.
- `proprium-de-tempore/in-tempore-quadragesimae/hebdomada-i-quadragesimae.json`
  - `hebdomada-i-quadragesimae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** God hath given His Angels charge over thee, to keep thee in all thy ways. **responsum:** They shall bear thee up in their hands, lest haply thou dash thy foot against a stone. **versus:** Thou shalt tread upon the adder and the cockatrice, the lion also, and the dragon shalt thou trample under feet.
  - `hebdomada-i-quadragesimae, feria, feria-v, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** I had been troubled, but that I knew thy mercy, O Lord; Thou hast said: I have no pleasure in the death of the wicked, but that he turn from his way and live. **responsum:** O Thou, Who didst call the Canaanite woman and the Publican unto repentance! **versus:** In the multitude of the sorrows within my heart, thy comforts delight my soul.
  - `hebdomada-i-quadragesimae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Behold, now is the acceptable time; behold, now is the day of salvation: let us approve ourselves in much patience, in much fasting; **responsum:** In the power of God, by the armour of righteousness. **versus:** In all things let us approve ourselves as the ministers of God, in much patience, in much fasting.
  - `hebdomada-i-quadragesimae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** In all things let us approve ourselves as the ministers of God, in much patience; **responsum:** That our ministry be not blamed. **versus:** Behold, now is the acceptable time; behold, now is the day of salvation: let us approve ourselves in much patience.
  - `hebdomada-i-quadragesimae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** The Priests shall pray, with fasting and with weeping, and shall say: **responsum:** Spare, O Lord, spare thy people; and give not thine heritage to destruction. **versus:** The Priests shall weep between the porch and the altar, and shall say:
  - `hebdomada-i-quadragesimae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Let us amend for the better in that wherein we have sinned unknowingly, or ever the day of death suddenly prevent us, and we seek a place of repentance, and find none. **responsum:** Give heed, O Lord, and have mercy upon us, for we have sinned against thee. **versus:** Help us, O God of our salvation, and for the glory of thy Name deliver us, O Lord.
  - `hebdomada-i-quadragesimae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Let the wicked forsake his way, and the unrighteous man his thoughts, and let him return unto the Lord, and He will have mercy upon him; **responsum:** For the Lord our God is gracious, and merciful, and repenteth Him of the evil. **versus:** The Lord hath no pleasure in the death of the wicked; but that he turn from his way and live.
  - `hebdomada-i-quadragesimae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** The season of the Fast openeth unto us the gates of heaven; let us enter thereon in prayer and supplication, **responsum:** That on the day when the Lord riseth again we may rejoice with Him. **versus:** In all things let us approve ourselves the ministers of God, in much patience.
  - `hebdomada-i-quadragesimae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Rend your hearts and not your garments, and turn unto the Lord your God; **responsum:** For He is gracious and merciful. **versus:** Let the wicked forsake his way, and the unrighteous man his thoughts, and let him return unto the Lord, and He will have mercy upon him.
  - `hebdomada-i-quadragesimae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Deal thy bread to the hungry, and bring the poor and the wanderer to thine house. **responsum:** Then shall thy light break forth as the morning, and thy righteousness shall go before thee. **versus:** When thou seest the naked, cover him; and hide not thyself from thine own flesh.
  - `hebdomada-i-quadragesimae, feria, feria-iv, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Shut up alms in the breast of the poor, and it shall plead for you with the Lord. **responsum:** For, as water will quench fire, so alms maketh an atonement for sins. **versus:** Give alms, and, behold, all things are clean unto you.
- `proprium-de-tempore/in-tempore-quadragesimae/hebdomada-ii-quadragesimae.json`
  - `hebdomada-ii-quadragesimae, feria, feria-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** While as Jacob went from Beersheba, and hasted unto Haran, the Lord spake unto him, saying **responsum:** The land whereon thou sleepest, to thee will I give it, and to thy seed. **versus:** He built an altar of stones unto the Name of the Lord, and poured oil upon the top of it; and God blessed him and said,:
  - `hebdomada-ii-quadragesimae, feria, feria-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** God appeared unto Jacob, and blessed him, and said I am the God of Bethel, where thou anointedst the pillar, and where thou vowedst a vow unto Me. **responsum:** I will make thee fruitful, and multiply thee. **versus:** Surely the Lord is in this place, and I knew it not.
  - `hebdomada-ii-quadragesimae, feria, sabbatum, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Father, I have sinned against heaven and before thee, and am no more worthy to be called thy son. **responsum:** Make me as one of thine hired servants. **versus:** How many hired servants of my father's have bread enough and to spare, and I perish with hunger! I will arise and go to my father and will say unto him
  - `hebdomada-ii-quadragesimae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Take thy weapons, thy quiver and thy bow, and bring me some of thy venison, that I may eat, **responsum:** and my soul may bless thee. **versus:** And when thou hast taken somewhat, make me thereof savoury meat, that I may eat.
  - `hebdomada-ii-quadragesimae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** See the smell of my son is as the smell of a field which the Lord hath blessed. May my God multiply thee as the sand of the sea. **responsum:** And give thee a blessing of the dew of heaven. **versus:** And God Almighty bless thee, and multiply thee.
  - `hebdomada-ii-quadragesimae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** God give thee of the dew of heaven and the fatness of the earth. Let people and nations serve thee. **responsum:** Be lord over thy brethren. **versus:** And let thy mother's sons bow down to thee.
  - `hebdomada-ii-quadragesimae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** As Jacob went out from his own land, he saw the glory of God, and said: How dreadful is this place. **responsum:** This is none other but the house of God and this is the gate of heaven. **versus:** Surely God is in this place, and I knew it not.
  - `hebdomada-ii-quadragesimae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** If the Lord my God will be with me, in this way that I go, and will keep me, and will give me bread to eat, and raiment to put on, and will bring me again safely. **responsum:** The Lord shall be my refuge, and this stone shall be a sign. **versus:** So Jacob rose up early in the morning, and took the stone that he had put for his pillow, and set it up for a pillar, and poured oil upon the top of it, and said:
  - `hebdomada-ii-quadragesimae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** The Lord shall be my God, and this stone, which I have set for a pillar, shall be called God's house, and of all that Thou shalt give me; **responsum:** I will offer tithes and peace-offerings to thee. **versus:** If I come again to my father's house in peace.
  - `hebdomada-ii-quadragesimae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** The Angel said unto Jacob **responsum:** Let me go, for the day breaketh. And he said: I will not let thee go, except thou bless me. And he blessed him there. **versus:** And when Jacob arose, behold there wrestled a man with him, until the breaking of the day and, when he saw that he prevailed not, he said unto him
  - `hebdomada-ii-quadragesimae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** I have seen God face to face; **responsum:** And my life is preserved. **versus:** And he said unto me: thy name shall be called no more Jacob, but Israel shall be thy name.
- `proprium-de-tempore/in-tempore-quadragesimae/hebdomada-iii-quadragesimae.json`
  - `hebdomada-iii-quadragesimae, feria, feria-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Take hence presents with you, and go unto the lord of the land, and when ye be come into his presence, bow yourselves to him to the earth. **responsum:** And my God give you mercy before the man, that he may send away again this your brother, and him which he keepeth in ward. **versus:** Take of the best fruits of the land in your vessels, and carry down the man a present.
  - `hebdomada-iii-quadragesimae, feria, feria-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Is this your younger brother, of whom ye spake unto me? God be gracious unto thee, my son. **responsum:** And he made haste, and entered into the house, and wept there, for his tears brake forth, and he could not refrain himself. **versus:** And Joseph lifted up his eyes, and saw his brother Benjamin, and his bowels yearned upon his brother.
  - `hebdomada-iii-quadragesimae, feria, feria-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Joseph said unto his eleven brethren: I am Joseph, whom ye sold into Egypt; is our father yet alive, the old man of whom ye spake unto me? **responsum:** Go, bring him down unto me, that he may live. **versus:** For these two years hath the famine been in the land; and yet there are five years, in the which there shall neither be earing nor harvest.
  - `hebdomada-iii-quadragesimae, feria, feria-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** They told Jacob, saying: thy son Joseph is yet alive, and he is governor over all the land of Egypt; and, when he heard it, his spirit revived, and he said **responsum:** It is enough; I will go, and see him before I die. **versus:** And when Jacob heard that his son yet lived, he was as one that awakeneth from a deep sleep, and said:
  - `hebdomada-iii-quadragesimae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** And when his brethren saw Joseph afar off, they said one to another: Behold, this dreamer cometh. **responsum:** Come, let us slay him; and we shall see what will become of his dreams. **versus:** And when his brethren saw that their father loved Joseph more than all his brethren, they hated him, and could not speak peaceably unto him; therefore they said:
  - `hebdomada-iii-quadragesimae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Judah said unto his brethren: Behold, the Ismaelites pass by; come, let us sell him, and let not our hands be defiled. **responsum:** For he is our flesh, and our brother. **versus:** What profit is it if we slay our brother, and conceal his blood? It is better to sell him.
  - `hebdomada-iii-quadragesimae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** When Jacob saw Joseph's coat he rent his clothes, and mourned; and he said: **responsum:** An evil beast hath devoured my son Joseph. **versus:** And his brethren took his coat, and sent it to his father and he knew it, and said:
  - `hebdomada-iii-quadragesimae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** When Joseph came into the land of Egypt, he heard a language that he understood not; his hands were burdened with labour; **responsum:** And his tongue spake wisdom among princes. **versus:** Whose feet they hurt with fetters; the iron entered into his soul, until the time that his word came
  - `hebdomada-iii-quadragesimae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** We are verily guilty concerning our brother, in that we saw the anguish of his soul, when he besought us, and we would not hear. **responsum:** Therefore is this distress come upon us. **versus:** And Reuben answered his brethren, saying Spake I not unto you, saying: Do not sin against the child; and ye would not hear?
  - `hebdomada-iii-quadragesimae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** And Reuben answered his brethren, saying: Spake I not unto you, saying: Do not sin against the child; and ye would not hear? **responsum:** Behold, his blood is required. **versus:** We are verily guilty concerning our brother, in that we saw the anguish of his soul, when he besought us, and we would not hear.
  - `hebdomada-iii-quadragesimae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Jacob lamented for his two sons, saying: Woe is me; I am bereaved of Joseph, for he is not; and afflicted because of Benjamin, because he is taken away for bread. **responsum:** I pray the King of heaven in my distress, that He may make me to see them yet again. **versus:** And Jacob cast him down upon his face upon the ground, and wept sore; and he prayed, saying:
- `proprium-de-tempore/in-tempore-quadragesimae/hebdomada-iv-quadragesimae.json`
  - `hebdomada-iv-quadragesimae, feria, feria-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** When ye be gone over Jordan, there shall ye build an altar unto the Lord **responsum:** Of whole stones; ye shall not lift up any iron tool upon them; and ye shall offer burnt-offerings thereon, and peace-offerings, unto your God. **versus:** When ye shall pass over Jordan unto the land which the Lord giveth you, there shall ye build an altar unto the Lord.
  - `hebdomada-iv-quadragesimae, feria, feria-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Hear, O Israel, the law of the Lord, and write it in thine heart as in a book **responsum:** And I will give unto thee a land flowing with milk and honey. **versus:** Take heed therefore, and hearken unto My voice and I will be an enemy unto thine enemies.
  - `hebdomada-iv-quadragesimae, feria, feria-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** As I was with Moses, so I will be with thee, saith the Lord. **responsum:** Be strong and of a good courage, and thou shalt bring My people into a land flowing with milk and honey. **versus:** Fear not, for I am with thee whithersoever thou goest I will not fail thee, nor forsake thee.
  - `hebdomada-iv-quadragesimae, feria, feria-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Why go ye about to kill Me, a Man That hath told you the truth? **responsum:** If I have spoken evil, bear witness of the evil; but if well, why smitest thou Me? **versus:** Many good works have I wrought among you; for which of those works go ye about to kill Me?
  - `hebdomada-iv-quadragesimae, feria, feria-iii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** I, even I, the Lord, have led you forty years in the wilderness, and your clothes are not waxen old upon you. **responsum:** I rained down manna upon you from heaven, and ye have forgotten Me, saith the Lord. **versus:** I led you forth out of the land of Egypt, and delivered you from the house of bondage.
  - `hebdomada-iv-quadragesimae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** The Lord spake unto Moses, saying: Go down now into Egypt, and say unto Pharaoh: **responsum:** Let My people go. And the heart of Pharaoh shall be hardened, that he will not let My people go but by a mighty hand. **versus:** The cry of the children of Israel is come unto Me, and I have seen their affliction: come now, therefore, and I will send thee unto Pharaoh, and thou shalt say unto him:
  - `hebdomada-iv-quadragesimae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Moses stood before Pharaoh, and said: Thus saith the Lord: **responsum:** Let My people go, that they may hold a feast unto Me in the wilderness. **versus:** The Lord God of the Hebrews hath sent me unto thee, saying:
  - `hebdomada-iv-quadragesimae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Let us sing unto the Lord, for He hath triumphed gloriously; the horse and his rider hath He thrown into the sea. **responsum:** The Lord is my strength and song, and He is become my salvation. **versus:** The Lord is a man of war; Almighty is His Name.
  - `hebdomada-iv-quadragesimae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Thy way is in the sea, and thy paths in the great waters. **responsum:** Thou led thy people like a flock, by the hand of Moses and Aaron. **versus:** Thou broughtest them through the Red Sea, and led them through much water.
  - `hebdomada-iv-quadragesimae, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** O Lord, Thou hast overwhelmed in the deep of the sea them which persecuted thy people; **responsum:** Even thy people which Thou led in the pillar of the cloud. **versus:** Thou led thy people like a flock, by the hand of Moses and Aaron.

  - `post-epiphaniam, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** O Lord, rebuke me not in thine anger, neither chasten me in thine hot displeasure. **responsum:** Have mercy upon me, O Lord, for I am weak. **versus:** Fearfulness and trembling are come upon me, and darkness hath overwhelmed me.
  - `post-epiphaniam, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** O God, Which satest in the throne judging right, be Thou a refuge for the poor, a refuge in times of trouble. **responsum:** For Thou alone beholdest mischief and spite. **versus:** The poor leaveth himself unto thee; Thou wilt be the helper of the fatherless.
  - `post-epiphaniam, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** The Lord is at my right hand, I shall never be moved. **responsum:** Therefore my heart is glad, and my tongue rejoiceth. **versus:** The Lord is the portion of mine inheritance, and of my cup.

  - `post-epiphaniam, dominica, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Unto thee, O Lord, do I lift up my soul. **responsum:** O my God, I trust in thee, let me not be ashamed. **versus:** O keep my soul and deliver me.
  - `post-epiphaniam, feria, feria-ii, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** O how great is thy goodness, O Lord **responsum:** Which Thou hast laid up for them that fear thee! **versus:** Which Thou, O Lord, hast wrought for them that trust in thee before the sons of men!
  - `post-epiphaniam, feria, feria-ii, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** O God, be Thou my helper. **responsum:** Neither leave me. **versus:** Nor forsake me O God of my salvation.
  - `post-epiphaniam, feria, feria-ii, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** I will bless the Lord at all times. **responsum:** His praise shall continually be in my mouth. **versus:** My soul shall make her boast in the Lord; the humble shall hear thereof and be glad.
  - `post-epiphaniam, feria, feria-iii, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** O God, give ear unto my tears; hold not thy peace, but, O, spare me! **responsum:** For I am a stranger with thee, and a sojourner. **versus:** Be pleased, O Lord, to deliver me; O Lord, look upon me to help me.
  - `post-epiphaniam, feria, feria-iii, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** The Lord hath set my feet upon a rock, and ordered my goings. **responsum:** And He hath put a new song in my mouth. **versus:** He heard my cry He brought me up also out of an horrible pit.
  - `post-epiphaniam, feria, feria-iii, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** I said: Lord, be merciful unto me. **responsum:** Heal my soul, for I have sinned against thee. **versus:** Deliver me from all mine iniquities, O Lord.
  - `post-epiphaniam, feria, feria-v, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** In thee, O God, do I put my trust; let me never be put to confusion, O Lord deliver me in thy righteousness, **responsum:** And cause me to escape. **versus:** Incline thine ear unto me, deliver me speedily.
  - `post-epiphaniam, feria, feria-v, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Let my mouth be filled with thy praise, that I may sing of thy glory, all the day long of thy greatness. Cast me not off in the time of old age; **responsum:** Forsake me not when my strength faileth. **versus:** My lips shall be fain when I sing unto thee.
  - `post-epiphaniam, feria, feria-v, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** My lips shall be fain when I sing unto thee; **responsum:** And my soul, which Thou, O Lord, hast redeemed. **versus:** My tongue shall also talk of thy righteousness, all the day long of thy praise.
  - `post-epiphaniam, feria, feria-vi, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** I will praise thee, O Lord my God, with all my heart, and I will glorify thy Name for evermore. **responsum:** For great is thy mercy, O Lord, toward me. **versus:** Thou art my God, and I will praise thee; Thou art my God, and I will exalt thee.
  - `post-epiphaniam, feria, feria-vi, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Great, O Lord, is thy mercy toward me. **responsum:** And Thou hast delivered my soul from the lowest hell. **versus:** In the day of my trouble I called upon thee, for Thou hast heard me.
  - `post-epiphaniam, feria, feria-vi, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** The Lord is my refuge. **responsum:** And my God is the stay of my trust. **versus:** He delivered me from the strongest of mine enemies, and the Lord was my stay.
  - `post-epiphaniam, feria, feria-iv, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Cut me not off in the midst of my sins. **responsum:** Nor keep thy wrath against me for my latter end. **versus:** Enter not into judgment with thy servant, O Lord.
  - `post-epiphaniam, feria, feria-iv, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** My heart is ready, O God, my heart is ready. **responsum:** I will sing and give praise to the Lord. **versus:** Awake up, my glory, awake, psaltery and harp! I will awake early.
  - `post-epiphaniam, feria, feria-iv, matutinum, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Unto thee, O my Strength, will I sing, for God is my defence, **responsum:** The God of my mercy. **versus:** I will be glad and rejoice in thee, I will sing praise to thy Name, O Thou Most High.

  - `corpus-christi, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** As the living Father hath sent Me, and I live by the Father, **responsum:** So he that eateth Me, even he shall live by Me. **versus:** With the bread of life and understanding hath the Lord fed him.

  - `post-pentecosten, temporale, dominica, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** The Lord That delivered me out of the mouth of the lion, and out of the paw of the bear **responsum:** He will deliver me out of the hand of mine enemies. **versus:** God hath sent forth His mercy and His truth, and delivered my soul from among the lion's whelps.

Alleluia. **versus:** The saving of our lives is in thy hand; only let us find grace in thy sight, and we will gladly be Pharaoh's servants.

  - `transfiguratio, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Behold what manner of love God the Father hath bestowed upon us, **responsum:** That we should be called, and should be, the sons of God. **versus:** We know that when He shall appear, we shall be like Him, for we shall see Him as He is.
  - `transfiguratio, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** They were abundantly satisfied with the fatness of thy house, and **responsum:** Thou madest them drink of the river of thy pleasures. **versus:** For with thee is the fountain of life, and in thy light shall we see light.

  - `immaculata-conceptio-bmv, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Come unto me all ye that be desirous of me **responsum:** And I will declare what God hath done for my soul. **versus:** As the Lord liveth, by me He hath fulfilled His mercy.

For thy faith hath helped her, and, behold, she is made whole; **responsum:** Because thou hast made in thy virginity a pleasant dwelling-place for thy God. **versus:** Even as Christ hath by me glorified Catania, so by thee shall He glorify Syracuse.

- `proprium-sanctorum/februarius/agatha.json`
  - `agatha, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** While the blessed Agatha was being grievously tortured in the breasts, she said to the judge: **responsum:** Thou foul, cruel, and bloody tyrant, art thou not ashamed to do this to me, having thyself sucked at a mother's breast? **versus:** I have breasts within, which have been the Lord's from my childhood, and them thou canst not mangle.

  - `agatha, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Who art thou, who comest to heal my wounds? I am an Apostle of Christ have no fear of me, my daughter He hath sent me to thee; **responsum:** Whom thy soul and thy pure heart love. **versus:** For I am His Apostle, and I bid thee know that thou art healed in the Name of Him.
  - `agatha, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** But by the Lord's help I will continue to acknowledge Him Who hath saved me **responsum:** And strengthened me. **versus:** I thank thee, O my Lord Jesus Christ, because Thou hast sent thine Apostle unto me to heal my wounds.
  - `agatha, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** He hath cured me Who hath sent His Apostle Peter to the prison to strengthen me, after I was ordered to be put on the rack. **responsum:** Because of my chastity, help me, O Lord my God, for they are torturing my breasts. **versus:** He hath vouchsafed to heal me of all my wounds, and to put new paps on my breasts.
  - `agatha, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** O Lord, Thou hast seen and known how I have fought, and how I have run in the race; but, because I would not obey the magistrates **responsum:** They ordered me to be tortured in the breasts. **versus:** Because of truth, and meekness, and righteousness,
  - `agatha, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** When the blessed Agatha came into the prison, she stretched forth her hands to God, and said O Lord, Who hast made me to overcome the cruelty of the executioners **responsum:** May it please thee that I should attain unto thy mercy. **versus:** O Lord, Who hast made me, Who hast taken from me all love for this present world, and Who hast saved my body from pollution,
  - `agatha, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** I have used no earthly medicine for my body, but I have for a Master, Christ Jesus **responsum:** By Whose Word alone all things are made. **versus:** He hath vouchsafed to heal me of all my wounds, and to put new paps on my breasts, and upon Him will I call, even upon the Living God.

  - `purificatio-bmv, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Make ready thy chamber, O Zion, to receive Christ thy King **responsum:** Even that Child Whom a Virgin bore, and remained a Virgin as before the fruit of her womb and the God of her soul. **versus:** Simeon took the Child up in his arms, and blessed God.
  - `purificatio-bmv, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** When the days of Mary's purification according to the law of Moses were accomplished, they brought Jesus to Jerusalem to present Him to the Lord; **responsum:** As it is written in the law of the Lord: Every male that openeth the womb shall be called holy unto the Lord. **versus:** They offered for Him unto the Lord a pair of turtle-doves or two young pigeons.
  - `purificatio-bmv, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** They offered for Him unto the Lord a pair of turtle-doves, or two young pigeons **responsum:** As it is written in the law of the Lord. **versus:** And when the days of Mary's purification according to the law of Moses were accomplished, they brought Him to Jerusalem, to present Him to the Lord.
  - `purificatio-bmv, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Simeon was just and devout, waiting for the redemption of Israel **responsum:** And the Holy Ghost was upon him. **versus:** It was revealed unto him by the Holy Ghost, that he should not see death, before he had seen the Lord's Christ.
  - `purificatio-bmv, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** It was revealed unto Simeon by the Holy Ghost that he should not see death before he had seen the Lord's Christ; **responsum:** And he blessed God, and said: Lord, now lettest Thou thy servant depart in peace, for mine eyes have seen thy salvation. **versus:** When His parents brought in the Child Jesus, to do for Him after the custom of the law, then took he Him up in his arms.
  - `purificatio-bmv, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Simeon took Jesus up in his arms, and cried out, and said: **responsum:** Verily Thou art a light to lighten the Gentiles, and the Glory of thy people Israel. **versus:** When His parents brought in the Child Jesus, then took he Him up in his arms, and blessed God, and said:
  - `purificatio-bmv, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** The old man bore the Child, but the Child was the old man's King; **responsum:** Even that Child Whom a virgin bore, and remained a virgin as before; and when that virgin had brought Him into the world, she fell down and worshipped Him. **versus:** Simeon took the Child up in his arms, and gave thanks, and blessed the Lord.
- `proprium-sanctorum/januarius/agnes.json`
  - `agnes, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Let us keep with joy and gladness the feast of this most saintly maiden, let us call to mind the holy passion of the blessed Agnes in her thirteenth year she conquered, losing death and finding life **responsum:** Because she loved the Only Giver of Life. **versus:** Reckoned but a child in this world, she understood more than the ancients.
  - `agnes, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** My right hand and my neck hath He clasped with precious stones, and put pearls beyond price in mine ears, **responsum:** And hath crowned me with the bright blossoms of the eternal spring-time. **versus:** He hath sealed me in my forehead, that I may let in no other lover but Him.
  - `agnes, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Christ is my Lover, and I am entering with Him into the marriage-chamber. His Mother is a Virgin, and His Father knoweth not a woman. The instruments of His music sound sweetly together in my ears. **responsum:** If I love Him I shall be chaste, if I touch Him I shall be clean, if I embrace Him I shall be a virgin indeed. **versus:** He hath espoused me with a betrothal ring, and adorned me with a necklace of great price.
  - `agnes, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** The Lord hath clothed me with the garments of salvation, and covered me with the robe of righteousness: **responsum:** And hath set a crown upon my head, as the crown of his bride. **versus:** He hath put pearls beyond price in mine ears, and hath crowned me with bright blossoms of the eternal springtime.
  - `agnes, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Honey and milk have I received from his mouth, **responsum:** And his Blood is red upon my cheeks. **versus:** He hath shewn me countless treasures, and hath sworn to endow me therewith.
  - `agnes, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** His holy Body is already united to me, and his Blood is red upon my cheeks: **responsum:** He it is whose Mother is a virgin, and whose Father hath not known a woman. **versus:** I am wedded to the Lord of Angels, at whose beauty the sun and the moon do wonder.
  - `agnes, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** I am wedded to the Lord of Angels, at Whose beauty the sun and the moon do wonder. **responsum:** I keep my troth to Him alone, and commit me altogether to Him. **versus:** My right hand and my neck hath He clasped with precious stones, and put pearls beyond price in mine ears.
  - `agnes, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** O Thou Who Alone art Almighty! Who Alone art to be adored! Who Alone art to be worshipped! Who Alone art to be feared! I bless thee, **responsum:** Because that through thine Only-begotten Son Thou hast delivered me from the threatenings of wicked men, and hast made me to walk with clean feet through the filthy haunts of devils. **versus:** My lips praise thee, my heart and all that is within me do long after thee.
- `proprium-sanctorum/januarius/cathedra-petri.json`
  - `cathedra-petri, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Peter, lovest thou Me? Lord, Thou knowest that I love thee. **responsum:** Feed My sheep. **versus:** Simon, son of Jonas, lovest thou Me more than these? Lord, Thou knowest that I love thee.
  - `cathedra-petri, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** The Lord hath chosen thee for a priest unto Himself, to offer up unto Him **responsum:** The sacrifice of praise. **versus:** Offer unto God thanksgiving, and pay thy vows unto the Most High.
- `proprium-sanctorum/januarius/conversio-pauli.json`
  - `conversio-pauli, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** He That wrought effectually in Peter to the Apostleship, the Same was mighty in me toward the Gentiles, **responsum:** And they perceived the grace that was given unto me of the Lord Christ. **versus:** The grace of God which was bestowed upon me was not in vain, but His grace abideth ever in me.
  - `conversio-pauli, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** I have fought a good fight, I have finished my course, I have kept the faith; **responsum:** Henceforth there is laid up for me a crown of righteousness. **versus:** I know Whom I have believed, and am persuaded that He is able to keep that which I have committed unto Him against that day.
  - `conversio-pauli, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** There is laid up for me a crown of righteousness: **responsum:** Which the Lord, the righteous Judge, shall give me at that day. **versus:** I know Whom I have believed, and am persuaded that He is able to keep that which I have committed unto Him against that day.
  - `conversio-pauli, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** O Holy Apostle Paul, thou art a chosen vessel unto God, to preach the Gospel throughout the whole world **responsum:** Through whom all nations have known the grace of God. **versus:** Pray for us to God Who hath chosen thee.
  - `conversio-pauli, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** By the grace of God I am what I am. **responsum:** And His grace which was bestowed upon me was not in vain, but abideth ever in me. **versus:** He that wrought effectually in Peter to the Apostleship, the Same was mighty in me toward the Gentiles.
  - `conversio-pauli, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Saul, who also is called Paul, was made a great preacher **responsum:** And being of God increased the more in strength, he confounded the Jews. **versus:** Proving that This is very Christ, the Son of God.
  - `conversio-pauli, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** O Holy Apostle Paul, Preacher of the truth, and teacher of the Gentiles, **responsum:** Pray for us to God, Who hath chosen thee, that we may be made worthy of the grace of God. **versus:** O Holy Apostle Paul, thou art a chosen vessel unto God, and a Preacher of the truth.
- `proprium-sanctorum/januarius/nomen-jesu.json`
  - `nomen-jesu, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Behold, thou shalt conceive, and bring forth a Son, and shalt call His Name Jesus **responsum:** For He shall save His people from their sins. **versus:** His Name was called Jesus, which was so named of the Angel, before He was conceived in the womb.
  - `nomen-jesu, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Blessed is thy Name, O God of our fathers, for in wrath Thou wilt remember mercy. **responsum:** And in the time of tribulation Thou forgivest the sins of them that call upon thee. **versus:** And blessed be thy glorious Name for ever, O Thou Who only doest wondrous things!
  - `nomen-jesu, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** I will praise thy Name continually; **responsum:** Yea, I will praise it with thanksgiving. **versus:** I will be glad and rejoice in thee I will sing praise to thy Name, O Thou Most High.
  - `nomen-jesu, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Let them that know thy Name put their trust in thee **responsum:** For Thou, Lord, hast not forsaken them that seek thee. **versus:** Arise, O Lord, help us, and redeem us for thy Name's sake.
  - `nomen-jesu, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Let us praise thy great Name, **responsum:** For it is terrible and holy. **versus:** Some trust in chariots, and some in horses, but we will call upon the Name of the Lord our God.
  - `nomen-jesu, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** O Lord, let all those that put their trust in thee rejoice, let them ever shout for joy because Thou dwellest in them **responsum:** Let them also that love thy Name be joyful in thee. **versus:** They shall walk, O Lord, in the light of thy countenance; and in thy Name shall they rejoice all the day.
  - `nomen-jesu, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Sorrow and trouble did I find. **responsum:** Then called I upon the Name of the Lord. **versus:** The Name of the Lord is a strong tower; I trusted in Him and I am helped.
  - `nomen-jesu, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** O Lord, I will wait on thy Name. **responsum:** For it is good before thy Saints. **versus:** To give thanks unto thy Holy Name, and to triumph in thy praise.
- `proprium-sanctorum/julius/elisabeth-portugalliae.json`
  - `elisabeth-portugalliae, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Born daughter of a kingly race, in heaven standeth Isabel victorious, clad in a vesture wrought about with three diverse colours of grace. **responsum:** For she hath left upon earth unto all them that come after her a noble example of a woman in three states of life. **versus:** As.a virgin, as a wife, and as a widow, she walked undented.
  - `elisabeth-portugalliae, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** She opened her mouth with wisdom, **responsum:** And in her tongue was the law of kindness. **versus:** For thou hast prevented her with the. blessings of sweetness.
  - `elisabeth-portugalliae, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Thou, O Lord, hast given her her heart's desire, **responsum:** And hast not withholden the request of her lips. **versus:** Thou hast compassed her with thy favour as with a shield.
  - `elisabeth-portugalliae, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** The kingdom of this world and all the beauty of life I have esteemed as nothing, for the excellency of the love of Jesus Christ my Lord **responsum:** Whom, having seen, I loved Whom, having believed, I longed after. **versus:** My heart is overflowing with a good matter I speak of my works unto the King.
- `proprium-sanctorum/julius/maria-magdalena-poenitens.json`
  - `maria-magdalena-poenitens, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Mary Magdalene and the other Mary went very early to the sepulchre. **responsum:** That Jesus whom ye seek, is not here: for He is risen, as He said: He goeth before you into Galilee; there shall ye see Him. **versus:** And very early in the morning, the first day of the week, they came unto the sepulchre, at the rising of the sun; and, entering into the sepulchre, they saw a young man sitting upon the right side, who saith unto them:
  - `maria-magdalena-poenitens, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Rejoice with me, all ye that love the Lord, for I sought Him and He hath appeared unto me **responsum:** And while as I was weeping at the Sepulchre, I saw the Lord. **versus:** When His disciples were gone away, I tarried still; and the fire of love in my heart yearned after Him.
  - `maria-magdalena-poenitens, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** They have taken away my Lord, and I know not where they have laid Him. The Angels say unto her: Woman, why weepest thou? He is risen, as He said. **responsum:** He goeth before you into Galilee; there shall ye see Him. **versus:** And as she wept, she stooped down and looked into the Sepulchre, and saw two Angels in white, sitting; and they say unto her:
- `proprium-sanctorum/julius/pretiosissimus-sanguis.json`
  - `pretiosissimus-sanguis, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Jesus also, that He might sanctify the people with His own Blood, suffered without the gate. **responsum:** Let us go forth therefore unto Him without the camp, bearing His reproach. **versus:** Ye have not yet resisted unto blood, striving against sin.
  - `pretiosissimus-sanguis, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Moses took the blood, and sprinkled all the people; **responsum:** Saying: This is the blood of the Testament which God hath enjoined unto you. **versus:** Through faith he kept the Passover, and the sprinkling of blood, lest he that destroyed the first-born should touch them.
  - `pretiosissimus-sanguis, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Pass the time of your sojourning here in fear, forasmuch as: **responsum:** Ye know that ye were not redeemed with corruptible things, as silver and gold **versus:** But with the Precious Blood of Christ, as of a lamb without spot.
  - `pretiosissimus-sanguis, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Ye are bought with a great price, therefore: **responsum:** Glorify God and bear Him in your body. **versus:** Ye are bought with a price; be not ye the servants of men.
  - `pretiosissimus-sanguis, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** God commendeth His love toward us: **responsum:** In that, while we were yet sinners, in due time Christ died for us. **versus:** Much more then being now justified by His Blood, we shall be saved from wrath through Him.
  - `pretiosissimus-sanguis, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** This is He That came by Water and Blood, even Jesus Christ; **responsum:** Not by Water only, but by Water and Blood. **versus:** In that day there shall be a fountain opened to the house of David and to the inhabitants of Jerusalem, for sin and for uncleanness.
  - `pretiosissimus-sanguis, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** God hath predestinated us unto the adoption of children by Jesus Christ; **responsum:** In Whom we have redemption through His Blood. **versus:** The forgiveness of sins, according to the riches of His grace, wherein He hath abounded toward us.
- `proprium-sanctorum/julius/visitatio.json`
  - `visitatio, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Rise up, make haste, my love my fair one and come away; For, lo, the winter is past, the rain is over and gone. **responsum:** The voice of the turtle-dove is heard in our land. **versus:** Mary entered into the house of Zacharias and saluted Elizabeth.
  - `visitatio, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Elizabeth was filled with the Holy Ghost and she spake out with a loud voice, and said: Blessed art thou among women, and blessed is the Fruit of thy womb; **responsum:** And whence is this to me, that the mother of my Lord should come to me? **versus:** For, lo, as soon as the voice of thy salutation sounded in mine ears, the babe leaped in my womb for joy.
  - `visitatio, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Behold, he cometh leaping upon the mountains, skipping upon the hills; **responsum:** My beloved is like a gazelle or a young roe-buck! **versus:** He rejoiceth as a giant to run his course, his going-forth is from the end of the heavens.
- `proprium-sanctorum/junius/joannes-paulus.json`
  - `joannes-paulus, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** These are two men full of mercy, who stand in the presence of the Lord, **responsum:** The Lord of the whole earth. **versus:** These are two olive trees, and two candlesticks, giving light in the presence of the Lord.
- `proprium-sanctorum/junius/nativitas-joannis-baptistae.json`
  - `nativitas-joannis-baptistae, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** There was a man sent from God, whose name was John. **responsum:** The same came for a witness, to bear witness of the Light, to make ready a people prepared for the Lord. **versus:** John was in the wilderness, preaching the baptism of repentance.
  - `nativitas-joannis-baptistae, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Elizabeth, the wife of Zacharias, was the mother of a mighty man, even of John the Baptist, the Forerunner of the Lord, **responsum:** who made straight in the desert an highway for the Lord. **versus:** There was a man sent from God, whose name was John.
  - `nativitas-joannis-baptistae, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Before I formed thee in the belly I knew thee; and before thou camest forth out of the womb I sanctified thee; **responsum:** And I ordained thee a Prophet unto the nations. **versus:** A man beloved of God and men, he was had in honour.
  - `nativitas-joannis-baptistae, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** The Angel of the Lord was sent down unto Zacharias, saying: Thou shalt beget a son in thine old age; **responsum:** and his name shall be called John the Baptist. **versus:** The child shall be great in the sight of the Lord; for His hand is with him.
  - `nativitas-joannis-baptistae, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** This is the well-beloved Fore-runner, a burning and a shining light before the Lord. **responsum:** For even this is John, who made straight in the desert an highway for our God. This also is he which bare witness, saying: Behold the Lamb of God! And his light lightened the minds of men. **versus:** He shall go before Him in the spirit and power of Elias.
  - `nativitas-joannis-baptistae, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** They made signs to his father, how he would have him called; and he asked for a writing-table, and wrote, saying: **responsum:** His name is John. **versus:** The mouth of Zacharias was opened, and he prophesied, saying:
  - `nativitas-joannis-baptistae, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** The Fore-runner of the Lord cometh, to whom He Himself bare witness, saying: **responsum:** Among them that are born of women there hath not risen a greater than John the Baptist. **versus:** A Prophet? Yea, and much more than a Prophet. This is he of whom the Saviour saith:
  - `nativitas-joannis-baptistae, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** The Angel Gabriel appeared unto Zacharias, and said thy wife Elizabeth shall bear thee a son, and thou shalt call his name John; **responsum:** And many shall rejoice at his birth. **versus:** For he shall be great in the sight of the Lord, and shall drink neither wine nor strong drink.
- `proprium-sanctorum/junius/petrus-paulus.json`
  - `petrus-paulus, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Simon Peter, before I called thee out of the ship, I knew thee, and appointed thee for a ruler over My people. **responsum:** And I have given unto thee the keys of the kingdom of heaven. **versus:** Whatsoever, thou shalt bind on earth, shall be bound in heaven; and whatsoever thou shalt loose on earth, shall be loosed in heaven.
  - `petrus-paulus, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Simon Peter, if thou lovest Me, feed My sheep. Lord, Thou knowest that I love thee; **responsum:** I will lay down my life for thy sake. **versus:** If I should die with thee, I will not deny thee.
  - `petrus-paulus, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Thou art Peter, and upon this rock I will build My Church, and the gates of hell shall not prevail against it. **responsum:** And I will give unto thee the keys of the kingdom of heaven. **versus:** Whatsoever thou shalt bind on earth, shall be bound in heaven; and whatsoever thou shalt loose on earth, shall be loosed in heaven.
  - `petrus-paulus, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Lord, if it be Thou, bid me come unto thee on the water; **responsum:** And Jesus stretched forth His Hand, and caught him, and said unto him: O thou of little faith, wherefore didst thou doubt? **versus:** But when he saw the wind boisterous, he was afraid and, beginning to sink, he cried, saying: Lord, save me!
  - `petrus-paulus, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Arise, O Peter! Cast thy garment about thee, gird thee with strength for the saving of the nations. **responsum:** The chains are fallen off from thine hands. **versus:** The Angel of the Lord came upon him, and a light shined in the prison and he smote Peter on the side and raised him up, saying: Arise up quickly.
  - `petrus-paulus, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Thou art the Shepherd of the sheep, and the Prince of the Apostles, and unto thee hath God given all the kingdoms of the world. **responsum:** Therefore unto thee hath He given the keys of the kingdom of heaven. **versus:** Whatsoever thou shalt bind on earth shall be bound in heaven; and whatsoever thou shalt loose on earth shall be loosed in heaven.
  - `petrus-paulus, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Peter, I have prayed for thee, that thy faith fail not; **responsum:** And when thou art converted, strengthen thy brethren. **versus:** Flesh and blood hath not revealed it unto thee, but My Father Which is in heaven.
  - `petrus-paulus, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Jesus asked His disciples, saying: Who do men say that I, the Son of Man, am? Peter answered, and said: Thou art the Christ, the Son of the living God. **responsum:** And I say unto thee, that thou art Peter, and upon this rock I will build My Church. **versus:** Blessed art thou, Simon Bar-Jona, for flesh and blood hath not revealed it unto thee, but My Father Which is in heaven.

(Alleluia.) **versus:** Lord, send thy Holy Spirit from heaven the Spirit of wisdom and understanding.

- `proprium-sanctorum/majus/cor-jesus.json`
  - `cor-jesus, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** I am that Bread of life. Your fathers did eat manna in the wilderness, and are dead. **responsum:** This is the Bread Which cometh down from heaven, that a man may eat thereof, and not die. **versus:** I am the living Bread Which came down from heaven if any man eat of this Bread, he shall live for ever.
  - `cor-jesus, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Behold, when we shall see Him, He hath no form nor comeliness there is no beauty in Him this is He Which hath borne our griefs and carried our sorrows but He was wounded for our transgressions **responsum:** And with His stripes we are healed. **versus:** Surely He hath borne our griefs and carried our sorrows.
  - `cor-jesus, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Blessed be the Lord God of hosts, Who only doeth wondrous things. **responsum:** And blessed be His glorious Name for ever. **versus:** And let the whole earth be filled with His glory. Amen. Amen.
  - `cor-jesus, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** He that eateth My Flesh and drinketh My Blood, **responsum:** Dwelleth in Me, and I in him. **versus:** What nation is there so great, who hath gods so nigh unto them, as the Lord our God is to us
  - `cor-jesus, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** He hath been brought as a lamb to the slaughter, and while he was evil entreated he opened not his mouth: he was delivered up to death **responsum:** That he might quicken his people. **versus:** He hath poured out his soul unto death, and he was numbered with the transgressors.
  - `cor-jesus, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** Great is the Lord, and greatly to be praised; **responsum:** And His Wisdom is unsearchable. **versus:** Great is our Lord, and of great power, and His understanding is infinite.
  - `cor-jesus, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** As the living Father hath sent Me, and I live by the Father, **responsum:** So he that eateth Me, even he shall live by Me. **versus:** With the bread of life and understanding hath the Lord fed him.
  - `cor-jesus, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Our Shepherd, even the Fountain of living waters, is gone from us He passed away, and the sun was darkened. **responsum:** Our Saviour hath burst the gates and bars of death. **versus:** The bands of hell He hath utterly abolished, and hath done away the power of the devil.
- `proprium-sanctorum/majus/inventio-crucis.json`
  - `inventio-crucis, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i, in-tempore-paschali`: **responsum:** On whose boughs her dying Saviour shattered death and crushed the serpent. (Alleluia, Alleluia, Alleluia.)
  - `inventio-crucis, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii, in-tempore-paschali`: **responsum:** Sweetest wood, and sweetest iron, Sweetest weight is hung on thee. (Alleluia.)
  - `inventio-crucis, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii, in-tempore-paschali`: **responsum:** Whereon the Author of our salvation did by His Own death openly triumph over the death of all men. (Alleluia, Alleluia.)
  - `inventio-crucis, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i, in-tempore-paschali`: **responsum:** Who hath saved us and redeemed us. (Alleluia.)
  - `inventio-crucis, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii, in-tempore-paschali`: **responsum:** As by the serpent Moses reared, so by the Cross the sick are healed. (Alleluia, alleluia.)
  - `inventio-crucis, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-iii, in-tempore-paschali`: **responsum:** Then shall the secrets of our hearts be made manifest. (Alleluia, alleluia.)
  - `inventio-crucis, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i, in-tempore-paschali`: **responsum:** Thou alone wast counted worthy this world's ransom to uphold. (Alleluia.)
  - `inventio-crucis, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-ii, in-tempore-paschali`: **responsum:** That whosoever believeth in Him should not perish, but have everlasting life. (Alleluia.)
  - `inventio-crucis, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** Faithful Cross, above all other, one and only noble tree None in foliage, none in blossom, none in fruit thy peers may be **responsum:** Sweetest wood and sweetest iron, Sweetest weight is hung on thee. (Alleluia.) **versus:** Thou art higher than all cedars.
  - `inventio-crucis, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-iii`: **incipit:** This is that noble tree, planted in the midst of the garden **responsum:** Whereon the Author of our salvation did by His Own death openly triumph over the death of all men. (Alleluia, Alleluia.) **versus:** Even the Cross, whereof the glory is so excellent, and whereafter Helen, the mother of Constantine, did so diligently search until she found it.
  - `inventio-crucis, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** But us it behoveth to glory in the Cross of our Lord Jesus Christ, in Whom is our salvation, life, and resurrection **responsum:** Who hath saved us and redeemed us. (Alleluia.) **versus:** O Lord, we worship thy Cross, and make memorial of thy glorious passion.
  - `inventio-crucis, matutinum, nocturna-ii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** The Relique true from heaven revealed, hath now the Gospel's figure sealed **responsum:** As by the serpent Moses reared, so by the Cross the sick are healed. (Alleluia, alleluia.) **versus:** When the dead touch the Cross they arise, and the wonderful works of God are made manifest.
  - `inventio-crucis, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** Sweetest wood and sweetest iron, Sweetest weight is hung on thee **responsum:** Thou alone wast counted worthy this world's ransom to uphold. (Alleluia.) **versus:** The sign of the Cross shall be in heaven when the Lord cometh to judgment.
  - `inventio-crucis, matutinum, nocturna-iii, responsorium, responsorium-ii`: **incipit:** As Moses lifted up the serpent in the wilderness, even so must the Son of Man be lifted up **responsum:** That whosoever believeth in Him should not perish, but have everlasting life. (Alleluia.) **versus:** God sent not His Son into the world to condemn the world, but that the world through Him might be saved.
- `proprium-sanctorum/martius/annuntiatio-bmv.json`
  - `annuntiatio-bmv, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i, in-tempore-paschali`: **responsum:** Behold, thou shalt conceive and bring forth a son, and He shall be called the Son of the Highest. (Alleluia.)
  - `annuntiatio-bmv, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-ii, in-tempore-paschali`: **responsum:** The Holy Ghost shall come upon thee, and the power of the Highest shall overshadow thee; therefore also that Holy Thing Which shall be born of thee shall be called the Son of God. (Alleluia.)

  - `caecilia, matutinum, nocturna-i, responsorium, responsorium-i`: **incipit:** The musicians played, and the maiden Cecilia sang in her heart unto the Lord alone, saying: **responsum:** Lord, let my heart and my body be undefiled, that I be not ashamed. **versus:** She fasted and prayed for two days and three days together, committing the cause of her fear unto the Lord.
